## Currency orb drops

*Corruption plugin, ROADMAP L4, [#33](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/33). Krogon's decisions of 2026-11-19 (`docs/handoffs/claude-charming-dijkstra-sg16vv.md`, 3–10). Tested in-game alone (2026-11-26, all 19 steps); not yet with the group. Since the personal-loot round (2026-11-26, built, untested) the orbs a player rolls are locked to them for 60 s, and the rip chest is gone (below).*

**What it does:** the ten currency orbs drop in normal play, so the Riftforge's Forge tab has something to craft with.

- **Every orb can drop from all content** (Krogon: "All orbs should have a chance to drop from all content"), the Exalted Orb and the Mirror too. The Jeweller's Orb doesn't drop until sockets exist.
- **Corrupted bosses (Krogon, 2026-11-23, decision 26):** instead of the roll, each player nearby gets **one guaranteed orb**, picked with the table's odds (as Hoardfinder's).
- **Maps from creatures (decisions 19, 31):** beside the orb roll, each player nearby rolls for a map of the biome the creature died in: 1 in 830 (`loot_rules.csv` → `map_drop_one_in`, as common as a Regal Orb), times the orb column's multipliers. Only once a rip heart has been destroyed in the world, and only once that biome's boss is beaten (read from the boss prefab's own defeat key). The Ocean has no map.
- **Kills:** every creature kill (bosses too: a boss is one kill) rolls the orb table once **per player nearby** (the game's own nearby-players range, as for Magic and Rare gear), on the creature's owner, and the orbs drop on the ground beside the gear. Each orb rolls on its own, so a kill can give several orbs or (usually) none.
- **Chests:** a vanilla chest with its own loot (dungeons, locations, shipwrecks) rolls the orb table once when the game fills it, at ×1.5 (`dungeon_chest`). This happens once per chest, when it's first created: chests the world made before this version have already filled. Chests players build are left alone. **The rip heart** (since the personal-loot round, 2026-11-26: no chest any more, the heart spills its loot) rolls once per player near the rip, at the rip multiplier (`rip_tier_1`; maps have no tiers yet), plus a Magic/Rare gear roll per player and one guaranteed map; each player's orb roll drops as theirs for 60 s (see [Rip waves and loot](#rip-waves-and-loot-step-b)).
- **Gathering:** Hoardfinder's (pickaxe, was Stonecutter's) and of Hidden Riches (axe, was Resinous) give a 1–5% chance per harvest of one guaranteed orb, picked with the same odds as weights (Transmutation 43%, Augmentation 28%, Alchemy and Annulment 12% each, Regal 2.6%, Exalted 1.1%, Chance 1.1%, Divine 0.2%, Mirror about 1 in 1,000): see [Loot system](#loot-system) → gathering, on harvest. Since 2026-11-26 the orb drops to the player who swung the tool and is locked to them for 60 s (built, untested).
- **Rates** (`Corruption/Data/orbs.csv`, per roll, before multipliers): Transmutation 1 in 50, Augmentation 1 in 77, Alchemy 1 in 172, Annulment 1 in 172, Regal 1 in 830, Exalted 1 in 1,865, Chance 1 in 2,000, Divine 1 in 10,000, Mirror 1 in 21,400. Path of Exile's order (its leaked 2014 currency table) squeezed onto a log scale between our Magic rate (1 in 50) and Unique rate (1 in 2,500); the Divine then made rarer (Krogon, decision 15: "super strong orbs and should be quite rare"; was 1 in 2,500), and Chance rarer than Alchemy (decision 16: while no uniques exist it always gives a Rare, like Alchemy; was 1 in 112). At about 125 kills an hour (the pace the Unique rate assumes) that's about 6 orbs an hour per player.
- **Multipliers:** the `orb` column of `drop_modifiers.csv`, multiplied for every source that applies and capped at 100%: stars and rips as for gear (1 star ×1.25, 2 stars ×1.5, 3 stars ×2, rip tier 1 ×1.25 …), dungeon chests ×1.5 and corrupted bosses ×3 as for uniques.

**Console (testing):** `co_mapdrops [rolls]` says whether creatures where you stand can drop a map (rip cleared, the biome's boss beaten) and rolls the map drop many times; drops nothing. `co_orbroll [rolls] [source ...]` rolls the table (default 10,000 times, as an ordinary kill) and prints each orb's count next to the expected number. Drops nothing. Example: `co_orbroll 100000 star_2 rip_tier_1`.

**Config:** none new; `7 - Loot` → `Enabled` switches orb drops off with the rest of the loot. Rates are in the tables (rebuild after editing).

**Multiplayer:** kills roll on the creature's owner, chests on the game that creates them (the owner of the zone or the rip), gathering on the broken object's owner, so every drop is a normal synced item. No new messages. Not tried with two players.

**Known issues:** **Red Unity error from the beams' smoke (found 2026-11-27, fixed, look check pending):** Krogon's 1.0.31 log had 9 red errors "Particle Velocity curves must all be in the same mode", one per loot beam made (the heart's 3). In `Corruption/Content/PersonalLoot.cs` the smoke's `velocityOverLifetime.y` was a single constant while `x` and `z` were ranges; Unity needs the same mode on all three. It came from the 2026-11-26 "softer, smoke-like beams" change (PR #88). Fixed on `claude/festive-gates-79y1yr` (the rise is now `MinMaxCurve(rise, rise)`); Unity may have skipped the smoke's movement until now, so the wisps may look a little different; **passed** in Krogon's final play-test (2026-11-27, `mtf-3`: "beams are fine, no red errors"). Orbs weren't locked to the player who rolled them until the personal-loot round (2026-11-26, passed alone on 1.0.30, merged in PR #88): now kill, heart and Hoardfinder's/of Hidden Riches orbs are locked to their player for 60 s and have a beam ([Personal loot and loot beams](#personal-loot-and-loot-beams)). Dungeon chest orbs stay inside the chest, shared.

**Harmony patch targets:**

| Method | Patch | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `CharacterDrop.OnDeath` | Postfix (the existing loot one) | Also rolls the orb table per nearby player |
| `Container.AddDefaultItems` | Postfix | One orb roll (`dungeon_chest`) when a vanilla chest with default loot fills itself (the "skipped while a rip chest is placed" check went with the chest, 2026-11-26) |

## Riftforge crafting: shards, shattering and Affix Splinters

*Corruption plugin, design phase 7, ROADMAP L5 (second part), issue #29. Rules: `docs/corruption-design.md` → Corrupted gear, Affix Splinters and shattering, with Krogon's round brief and answers of 2026-11-10 (`docs/handoffs/claude-brave-einstein-xz357h.md`). Used from the Forge tab ([Riftforge workspace](#riftforge-workspace)); `co_craft` uses the same calls.*

**What it does in-game:**

- **Corrupting with a Corruption shard.** Right-click a shard in Currencies, click the gear in the forge slot. The **Corrupt item** dialog asks first (the same design as Shatter's, below): the gear's icon and name, "Cost: 1 Corruption shard (you have N)", the warning "This can permanently destroy the item.", **Possible outcomes** with their chances right-aligned, read from `corruption_outcomes.csv` (today Destroyed 25% · Unchanged and locked 25% · Gains one corrupted affix, and is locked 50%), and that anything that survives is locked for good, apart from the Mirror of Kalandra. **Cancel** changes nothing and uses nothing. **Corrupt** uses exactly one shard, also when the gear is destroyed.
  - **Destroyed:** the gear is gone, the forge slot empties: "Destroyed: the shard consumed <name>."
  - **Unchanged and locked:** no affix; the gear is now Corrupted: "Corrupted and locked; no affix added." (never shown as "no change").
  - **Corrupted affix:** one extra affix from the gear's normal pool, on top of the normal limit (a full Rare ends with 5, a Normal item with 1 and stays Normal). It may repeat a kind the gear already has. It is its own red line marked "(corrupted)". If it happens to complete a twin, the corrupted half rolls its value, then the two values are added with the 25% resonance bonus on top (Krogon, 2026-11-10: like a splinter; it never loses the item's value); the resonant line is marked corrupted.
  - Works on Normal, Magic and Rare gear. Refused, using nothing: "Already corrupted", "Uniques can't be corrupted", "Mirrored items can't be changed", "This item can't take affixes" (gear outside `loot_bases.csv`).
- **Corrupted affixes count beyond the caps.** For the capped stats (`affix_caps.csv`: movement speed, attack speed, pickup radius, each resistance, life steal) the ordinary affixes are added up and capped as before, **over everything worn**, and corrupted affixes are added on top. Corrupting an item never uncaps its ordinary affixes. Resistance never goes past 100% (past that a hit would heal). Limit: Skald's and Fenrir (attack-speed buffs from a parry or a kill) stay under the cap even from a corrupted line, because the buff carries one number.
- **Where caps are shown:** not in tooltips (Krogon, 2026-11-10: "the tooltip is already overly crowded"). A detailed character stats sheet will show each cap and how close you are: issue #60. The numbers are ready (`AffixTotals.Progress`, tested).
- **Shattering.** The Forge tab's **Shatter** button, on Magic or Rare gear. Free. **Its own dialog asks first** (Krogon's brief, 2026-11-10): the title "Shatter item", the gear's icon and rarity-coloured name, "Cost: Free", the warning "This permanently destroys the item." once, then **Potential splinters** as two columns, each affix as the Forge shows it (bullets, green ◆ and green text for resonant lines, value and range, wrapped lines indented under the text) with its chance right-aligned beside it (`shattering.csv`, 50%; a resonant line is one line), a short note ("Each affix rolls independently; you may recover none. Recovered splinters keep their exact values."), a **Don't warn me again** tickbox on its own row, and **Cancel** (left) and **Shatter** (right) in a footer of their own. Drawn on the game's normal wood panel (the inventory's own background, as the character sheet uses) with the message box's heading font, body font and buttons, over a plain dark backing so text never floats over the inventory; its height follows the content (`ForgeDialogLayout`, tested: nothing overlaps, the tickbox and the footer have their own rows) and the whole dialog shrinks if the screen is too small. Escape or Cancel changes nothing. Previews (not screenshots): `docs/images/forge_dialog_preview.png` (`tools/mockup_forge_dialogs.py`). **Shatter** destroys the gear and gives 0 or more **Affix Splinters**, each holding one line with its exact value (a resonant line: the combined value).
  - **Room is checked before anything is rolled**: one place per line the gear shows, in the Splinters tab or, when that is full, the Bag; the gear's own Bag cell counts as free (only when the gear sits in the Bag grid). Not enough: "Make room for up to X splinters." and nothing happens. Checked when the button is pressed and again on Yes, so a roll can never be turned down by filling the bag.
  - **One operation:** the gear goes and every splinter arrives, or nothing changes. If a splinter can't be stored after all, the gear is put back and no splinter stays. Nothing is ever dropped on the ground.
  - Refused: "Only Magic and Rare items shatter", "Uniques and corrupted items can't be shattered", "Mirrored items can't be changed", "This item has no affixes".
- **Affix Splinters**: stack 1, goes to the **Splinters** tab, through portals, into chests and to other players with its line (saved in the item's own data, `corruption.splinter`). The tooltip shows the line, whether it is a prefix or a suffix, and which gear takes it. Right-click one, click the gear: the line lands with its **exact value**, the splinter is used up, a Normal item becomes Magic. No box: nothing is destroyed and nothing is rolled.
  - **Look (Krogon approved 2026-11-10):** rough organic lumps after Path of Exile's catalysts, **a shape per kind of affix** (attack, elemental, defence, vitality, resistance, utility, mining, woodcutting, any-tool gathering) and **a colour per stat** (fire orange, frost blue, health red, …), with a faint glow and their own inventory icons. `Corruption/Data/splinter_looks.csv` gives each affix family its kind and colour (a test checks every affix has a row); `tools/make_splinters.py` makes the models and textures (`Corruption/Assets/Splinters/`, embedded); preview `docs/images/splinters_preview.png`. Each family is its own item, `CorruptionAffixSplinter_<family>` (tool affixes `CorruptionAffixSplinter_tool_<family>`), so a saved splinter keeps its item if colours change later. The plain `CorruptionAffixSplinter` (a tinted gem) is kept for a family with no look.
  - **Completing a twin (Krogon, 2026-11-10):** when the gear already has the other half, the two become one resonant line worth **the two values added, plus 25%** (Hale +14 and a splinter of the Bear +18 → +40). Always the same result. Two top halves reach exactly the resonant range's top.
  - **An already-resonant splinter** moves its exact value and only fits gear with no affixes.
  - Only on Normal and Magic gear within 2 affixes. Refused: "Splinters only go on Normal and Magic items", "Mirrored items can't be changed", "This item has no room for this splinter" (2 affixes already, or that side is taken), "This affix can't roll on this item" (wrong kind of gear), "This item already has that kind of affix", "This splinter's affix can't be read".
- **A Mirror's copy is locked like a corrupted item** (Krogon, 2026-11-10): no shard, no shattering, no splinter.
- **"Don't warn me again"**: both dialogs have the tickbox (Krogon, 2026-11-10: players do this many times and need a way to hide the panel once they know it). It counts only when the action is confirmed, and is remembered between sessions, separately for shards and for shattering; with it off, a shard works with one click and Shatter with one press.

**Config** (per player, not synced; BepInEx config `10 - Riftforge`): `WarnBeforeCorrupting` (default on), `WarnBeforeShattering` (default on). Switch them back on here.

**Console (devcommands), item in hotbar slot 1:** `co_craft corrupt` (uses a shard, no box), `co_craft shatter` (no box), `co_craft splinter` (the first splinter you carry), `co_craft give` (now also 5 shards), `co_craft list` (also lists shatter, corrupt and the first splinter).

**Known issues and limits:**
- **Play-test 2026-11-10/11 (single player): passed.** Shattering, splinters (refusals, a second affix, their shapes and colours), the three shard outcomes and their messages, both dialogs and their tickboxes (kept after a restart), the full-storage refusal and reconnecting: "these have all been checked, all working". Found and fixed on the way: the splinter shape files missing from the build, the dialogs' background (none, then a loading-screen picture), a stray gamepad hint on the buttons, font warnings in the log (that last fix not re-checked in the log). Not tried: multiplayer (`PLAYTEST.md` → Session 2, 2i). Checklist and feedback: `docs/playtests/claude-brave-einstein-xz357h.md`.
- The splinter colours are Claude's picks inside Krogon's approved idea (`status` `proposed` in `splinter_looks.csv`); change any colour there and rerun `tools/make_splinters.py`. Renaming an affix family would orphan its splinters (their item name uses the family).
- 68 splinter items render their icons when the game starts (a little more start-up time).
- Splinter weight 0.2 (the shard's number; no number of its own in the design; the Splinters tab is weightless anyway).
- Both confirmations are the mod's own dialog; mouse only. Play-test 2026-11-10: the first Shatter dialog had no background and the shard box's tickbox overlapped its text; both rebuilt as one dialog. Second look: the layout and tickbox were right, but the panel showed a loading-screen picture (a troll) and the buttons a stray "MISSING BUTTON DEF" gamepad hint; fixed (the inventory's own panel background; hints hidden), to re-test.
- A gear piece that was equipped when a failed shatter put it back comes back unequipped.
- Items saved by this build with a corrupted affix can't be read by older builds (the saved line starts with `~`); everyone needs the same build anyway.
- Whether EquipmentAndQuickSlots reports an equipped item as "in the Bag grid" isn't checked against its code; the safety net is the put-back above.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden:** none new. 68 new items through Jötunn (cloned from `GemstoneRed`, the orbs' base). Harmony: no new patch and no new target. The existing `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip` postfix adds the corrupted mark, the cap lines and a splinter's line; the existing loot patches that apply a capped stat (movement speed, resistances, life steal, pickup radius, attack speed, Foraging; targets listed under [Loot system](#loot-system)) now add corrupted affixes beyond the cap. The Forge tab's patches are unchanged.

## Talismans

*Corruption plugin, design phase 2.*

**What it does in-game:** Trophy talismans ("<creature> charm", lore: Ægishjálmur, the Helm of Awe).
- **Effect:** while worn, a talisman stops one creature family from targeting, chasing or hitting you. You can still hit them. Hitting one **provokes** it: that creature fights back for 30 s after your last hit; the rest of the family keeps ignoring you.
- **Scope:** only the wearer is protected; other players are unaffected. Bosses are never repelled.
- **Families** come from game data, not a hand-written list:
  - a base creature plus every creature whose prefab name extends it (Greydwarf → Greydwarf_Elite, _Shaman, _Frozen; Goblin → GoblinArcher, GoblinBrute, GoblinShaman; Skeleton → every skeleton variant);
  - only creatures with monster AI (no deer or hares);
  - quest and named minibosses excluded (configurable).
  - About 40 families in vanilla 1.0; the full list is logged at startup (`Registered N talismans: ...`).
- **Recipe** (per family): 3 of the family's trophy, 1 Chain, 10 Leather scraps, at the workbench. Like any vanilla recipe it appears once you've picked up all three. Chain comes from the Swamp, so talismans arrive mid-game.
- **Slots (changed 2026-10-28):** talismans have their own slots, **2 by default** (admin setting, 1–3), so you can wear **2 talismans and 1 vanilla trinket** at the same time. A talisman works while it sits in a talisman slot.
  - The slots are added to **EquipmentAndQuickSlots**' equipment panel through its slot API. Without that mod, talismans have no slot and do nothing (a warning is logged at startup).
  - The same talisman can't go into two slots (a copy adds nothing); different families stack.
- **Item:** not equippable (cloned from a vanilla trinket for its model, then made a plain item with no bonuses), so it never takes the vanilla trinket slot. The icon is the family's trophy.
- **Multiplayer:** the repel check runs on the machine that simulates the creature. Other games can't see what's in your talisman slots, so your game writes the talismans you wear onto your player (synced to everyone) twice a second, and the check reads them from there.
- **Fight-back time in multiplayer (review fix 2026-11-08; passed alone 2026-11-08, the owner change needs two players: Session 2, 2g):** the time a provoked creature fights back is written on the creature itself (synced to everyone), one entry per player (`corruption_provoked_<player id>`), as world time, which every game shares. So when the game running the creature logs out and another player's game takes it over, the creature keeps fighting back for the rest of the time; a new hit starts the time again; other players and other creatures of the family are unaffected. Before, the time lived only in the memory of the game running the creature, so a takeover ended it at once. Each provoking hit logs `Talisman: <creature> fights back at <player> for 30 s (run by this game).` on the game running the creature (for the multiplayer check).
- Talismans not working inside rips (design) comes with phase 4.

**How to use it:** Craft the charm for a creature family at the workbench and drag it into a Talisman slot in the EquipmentAndQuickSlots equipment panel.

**Config settings** (admin-only, synced from the server):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Enabled` | `true` | Off: charms can be crafted but do nothing |
| `Slots` | `2` | How many talisman slots work (1–3). Needs EquipmentAndQuickSlots |
| `ProvokeSeconds` | `30` | How long a creature you hit fights back after your last hit |
| `ExcludedCreatures` | `*Hildir*, GoblinBruteBros*, Charred_Melee_Dyrnwyn` | Creature prefab names never repelled (`*` wildcard). Restart to apply |

The family rules are unit-tested against the real 1.0 creature list (`TalismanFamiliesTests`).

**Known issues:**
- Play-test 2026-10-28 (with EquipmentAndQuickSlots, AdventureBackpacks and PlantEasily loaded): the two **Talisman** slots appear in the EquipmentAndQuickSlots equipment panel next to the Trinket slot, and the charm recipes show at the workbench. Two bugs found and fixed (awaiting re-test):
  - the charm's tooltip said "Trinket": that was the subtitle line copied from the vanilla trinket; it now says "Talisman: place in a Talisman slot";
  - the charm kept the trinket's **Adrenaline 50**: its bonuses were stripped using a list the game only fills once a player spawns, after charms are made, so nothing was stripped. Now stripped by field name.
  - Also fixed before it showed: charms picked up from the ground (or spawned with cheats) weren't recognised by the slots until relogging, because such items carry their own copy of the item data. Talismans are now recognised by the prefab they came from.
- Play-test 2026-10-28, after the fixes: **working as intended** (user). A Greydwarf charm in a Talisman slot shows the new subtitle and no Adrenaline, and the user confirmed the behaviour. Not yet covered: two charms at once plus a vanilla trinket, provoking, and multiplayer.
- **Play-test 2026-10-28 (single player): passed.** Two different charms plus a vanilla trinket work together; the same charm can't go in both slots; a hit creature fights back for about 30 s, then ignores you again; bosses ignore charms. Multiplayer still untested.
- In game, talismans are called "<creature> charm" (lore: Ægishjálmur charms); "talisman" is the slot and system name.
- Untested in-game.
- Families are by name, so some group widely: one Blob charm covers every ooze (tar, frost, lava...), and the Troll charm also covers Deep North frost trolls. Exclusions can split off individual creatures.
- A creature that already targets you when you put the charm on may keep following you, but can't hit you.
- Charm names use the creature's name in the game language at startup, with English text around it ("Greydwarf charm").
- The fight-back entries stay on the creature's saved data once written (one small number per player who provoked it); they do nothing once their time has passed.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `BaseAI.IsEnemy(Character, Character)` | Postfix | Returns false when a repelled creature asks about a wearer (and it isn't provoked). Never changes the answer when the player is the one asking, so player attacks are unaffected |
| `BaseAI.OnDamaged` | Prefix | Marks a creature as provoked when the wearer hits it (runs before monsters pick their target): on the creature's owner, writes the fight-back time for that player on the creature's ZDO |
| `Player.Update` | Postfix | Local player only, twice a second: writes the talismans in its talisman slots to its player object, for other games to read |

Also calls EquipmentAndQuickSlots' public API (`AddSlot`, `TryGetSlotItem`) to add and read the talisman slots; no patches on that mod.

**Conflicts:** other mods that patch `BaseAI.IsEnemy` (taming, faction or "friendly creature" mods) may interact. The postfix only ever turns `true` into `false` for this one case.

## Boss essences

*Corruption plugin, design phase 3.*

**What it does in-game:** Every boss drops **1 essence per kill** (every kill, not only the first). Essences are the map ingredient of the core loop: each boss's essence will craft maps into the corrupted version of its own biome at the Riftforge (phase 4; until then they have no use). They stack to 20, weigh 0.5, go through portals and can't be sold.

| Boss | Item | Look | Maps into |
|---|---|---|---|
| Eikthyr | `CorruptionEssence_eikthyr` | Pale ice-blue gem with lightning veins | Corrupted Meadows |
| The Elder (`gd_king`) | `CorruptionEssence_elder` | Amber sap gem with a wooden root wrapped round it | Corrupted Black Forest |
| Bonemass | `CorruptionEssence_bonemass` | Toxic green gem with bone-white flecks | Corrupted Swamp |
| Moder (`Dragon`) | `CorruptionEssence_moder` | Frosted white-blue gem with ice cracks | Corrupted Mountains |
| Yagluth (`GoblinKing`) | `CorruptionEssence_yagluth` | Ember-orange gem with a gold ring | Corrupted Plains |
| The Queen (`SeekerQueen`) | `CorruptionEssence_queen` | Violet chitin gem | Corrupted Mistlands |
| Fader | `CorruptionEssence_fader` | Molten orange-black gem | Corrupted Ashlands |
| Kall Fimbulbringer (`FrozenKing_p3`, the final phase) | `CorruptionEssence_kall` | Midnight-blue ice with glowing frost runes and a silver crown | Corrupted Deep North |

All eight share one egg-shaped gem model (`essence.obj`) with the boss's own texture, glow colour and, for the Elder, Yagluth and Kall, an extra part. The first seven follow the user's concept render (`docs/images/essences_reference.jpg`); Kall's look is ours (not in the render, awaiting the user's opinion); the in-game preview is `docs/images/essences_preview.png`, both made by `tools/make_essences.py`.

**How to use it:** Kill a boss. Test without fighting: `spawn CorruptionEssence_eikthyr` (or any item above).

**Data table:** [`Corruption/Data/essences.csv`](Corruption/Data/essences.csv): boss, name, description, glow colour, add-on part, `per_kill`, target biome. Compiled into the DLL, so **rebuild after editing**. `dotnet test` checks the file, that every boss is a boss in the 1.0.20 game data, and that every look has its model files. A new look needs its textures made by `tools/make_essences.py`.

**Config settings:**

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Enabled` (`4 - Essences`) | `true` | Admin-only. Bosses drop essences; off = no drops (essences already found stay) |
| `ShardGlowIntensity`, `ShardGlowRange` (`2 - Look`) | `2`, `2.5` | Shared with the shard: the ground glow of a dropped essence. Per player, restart to apply |

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-28 (single player): all passed.** All eight essences look right on the ground and in the inventory (models, icons, glow, the Elder's root, Yagluth's ring, Kall's crown); Eikthyr drops 1 essence on every kill; Kall's essence drops from the last phase only.
- The Hive (an unused, console-only boss in the game files) is deliberately left out (user, 2026-10-28).
- Kall Fimbulbringer's earlier phases (`FrozenKing`, `FrozenKing_p2`) don't drop it; only the final phase does, like vanilla's drops.
- If a custom look fails, that essence falls back to a tinted Bloodstone and logs an error; the item is always registered.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList` | Postfix | Adds the boss's essence (`per_kill`) to its drop list. A second postfix next to the shard one |

New items registered through Jötunn: `CorruptionEssence_<look>` for each table row (cloned from `GemstoneRed`).

## Maps and rips (step A)

*Corruption plugin, design phase 4, step A: travel only. Waves, maddened creatures and the end loot come in step B.*

**What it does in-game:**
- **Maps.** Eight maps, one per biome (Meadows map … Deep North map; until 2026-11-23 "Corrupted Meadows map …": "corrupted" maps come with the map tiers), crafted at the **Riftforge** from 1 corruption shard, 1 of that biome's boss essence and a stack of a biome material (Leather scraps 5, Greydwarf eye 5, Guck 2, Freeze gland 3, Needle 5, Carapace 16, Charred bone 8, Ice 5). Where each comes from: [Data table](#maps-and-rips-step-a) below. Like vanilla recipes, a map shows up once you've picked up all three. Maps don't stack, go through portals and can't be sold. **Icons** (2026-10-29): each map has its own icon, cut from the user's concept render (`docs/images/maps_reference.jpg`) by `tools/make_map_icons.py`: a hide in the biome's colour with one bold silhouette, a red tear and a bead in the essence's colour. On the ground a map is still a bundle of leather tinted in the essence's colour.
- **Opening a rip.** Use the map (right-click it in your inventory, or from the hotbar). Your game looks for land of that biome **you haven't explored** (your map, including what you've added from a cartography table), above water and as flat as the game requires for placing a location; the server then picks the first spot that is **96 m or more from anything a player placed** (`BaseDistance`; *changed 2026-11-26 (Krogon, decision 12): for every rip, replacing the old rule of a ward's radius from beds, wards and portals*, see [Map tiers and affixes](#map-tiers-and-affixes)). The map is used up and a red stone portal, the **rip**, opens 4 m in front of you. If nothing suitable is found you're told why and keep the map. *Changed 2026-11-27: the map's tier and affixes travel with the request, and when no unexplored land of the biome is left the rip falls back to explored land under the same 96 m rule.*
- **Through the rip.** Walk in (or press E) to travel to the landing spot. Vanilla portal rules apply: no ores or metals. Anyone can follow while it's open. *Changed 2026-10-29:* the **rip heart** (a giant glowing corruption shard) is no longer beside the landing spot but 96–128 m away: see [Rip heart search and corrupted wishbone](#rip-heart-search-and-corrupted-wishbone).
- **Getting home.** Destroy the heart (it has a greydwarf nest's health unless `HeartHealth` is set) to open a second rip beside it that leads back to the first. Both rips and the heart close after `OpenDays` (1 in-game day, as in the design; sleeping skips time). No other way back: bring a portal kit or walk.

**How to test:**
- `spawn CorruptionMap_Meadows` (or `_BlackForest`, `_Swamp`, `_Mountain`, `_Plains`, `_Mistlands`, `_AshLands`, `_DeepNorth`), then right-click it.
- Crafting: a Riftforge, a shard, an essence and the material.

**Data table:** [`Corruption/Data/maps.csv`](Corruption/Data/maps.csv): recipe per biome. Compiled into the DLL, so **rebuild after editing**. `dotnet test` checks it, that every essence exists and every material is a game item. Where the numbers come from (2026-10-28, from the 1.0.20 game data):
- **Shards and essence:** 1 each, from the design doc's map recipe.
- **Material:** the one the design doc names (Black Forest, Swamp, Mountains, Plains). Where it offers two, the one that drops earliest, as the design asks: Carapace (guaranteed from Seekers) over Soft tissue (25% from Dvergers); Charred bone (guaranteed from the Charred) over Flametal (mining and smelting). Where it names none (Meadows, Deep North): the guaranteed non-food drop of the weakest creature found only in that biome: Leather scraps (Boar), Ice (Frozen greydwarf, Deep North skeleton).
- **Amount:** the median amount of that material in vanilla recipes and building pieces, rounded up: what the game itself typically asks for.

**Config settings** (admin-only, synced from the server):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Enabled` | `true` | Maps open rips; off = using a map does nothing (the map is kept) |
| `OpenDays` | `1` | How long a rip stays open, in in-game days |
| `BaseDistance` | `0` | Rips never open (heart or landing spot) within this many metres of anything a player placed. 0 = 96 m (the waves' range; Krogon, 2026-11-26). *Until 2026-11-27: of a bed, ward or portal, 0 = the ward's radius* |
| `HeartHealth` | `0` | Health of the rip heart. 0 = the greydwarf nest's own health |

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-28 (single player):** opening a rip, landing on the ground beside the heart, destroying the heart, the exit rip leading home, a second biome (Plains) and the map recipe at the Riftforge all work. Two bugs, **fixed, to re-test**: the portal hover showed `{0}` instead of the biome and time left (our texts used `{0}`; the game fills in `$1`), and a red console error (`NullReferenceException` in `LightFlicker.Awake`) when the heart loaded (the nest's light-flicker script stayed after its light was removed). Not yet tested: anything in multiplayer.
- **Re-test 2026-10-28: both fixes confirmed** (hover shows the biome and time left; no errors). A `GetBiome error Meadows -> BlackForest` warning in the log is the game's own, logged when standing on a biome border.
- **Rips last a full in-game day:** sleeping only skips the night, so a rip opened in the evening is still open the next morning (seen 2026-10-28; the user is fine with it).
- The landing spot comes from world generation. Locations (villages, altars) aren't avoided yet; step B adds checks once the area has loaded.
- **Map icons tested in game 2026-10-29:** all eight show in the inventory and look good (user). The look on the ground is still the tinted leather bundle.
- With AdventureBackpacks' backpack open, right-clicking a map moves it (AB's quick transfer) instead of using it. Close the backpack first.
- A rip opened by a player who then leaves: its objects close when someone is next near them after the time is up.
- **Changed 2026-11-27 (built, untested):** `Humanoid.UseItem` also passes the exact map used and checks it is in the inventory used; `Player.UpdateTeleport` also shows the 15-second "The map falls silent. Try again."; with the Forge tab open a right-clicked map goes into the forge instead of opening a rip. Details in [Map tiers and affixes](#map-tiers-and-affixes).

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Humanoid.UseItem` | Prefix | Using a map from the player's own inventory opens a rip instead of vanilla "use on what?". Steps aside when another mod already handled the use |
| `Player.UpdateTeleport` | Postfix | After a rip teleport finishes, puts the player on the ground at the destination |
| `Game.Start` | Postfix | Registers the rip messages between players' games and the server (`Corruption_RipRequest`, `Corruption_RipReply`) |

New items: `CorruptionMap_<Biome>` (cloned from `LeatherScraps`). New objects: `corruption_rip` (cloned from `portal_stone`, falling back to `portal_wood`; vanilla portal logic, building and damage removed) and `corruption_rip_heart` (cloned from `Spawner_GreydwarfNest`; spawning and drops removed, the shard model at 6× size).

## Rip waves and loot (step B)

*Corruption plugin, design phase 4, step B. Every number comes from the game data or the user (sources below).*

**What it does in-game:**
- **Waves.** When a player comes within 96 m of a rip heart (the raids' own range), the rip "tears open": the raid's start message shows, and every creature already nearby turns **maddened** and hunts you. Then waves keep coming **while the heart stands**.
- **Which creatures and how many:** the biome boss's own **army raid**, exactly as the game defines it: Meadows = Eikthyr's army (boars, necks), Black Forest = the Elder's (greydwarfs, greylings, shamans, brutes), Swamp = Bonemass's (draugr, skeletons), Mountains = Moder's (drakes), Plains = Yagluth's (fulings, brutes, shamans), Mistlands = the Queen's (gjall, ticks, seekers), Ashlands = Fader's (charred and their spawner stones), Deep North = Kall's (elakingar, Jotuns). Each raid entry's own limit on how many are alive, spawn interval, chance and group size apply; groups appear 40–80 m from a player, as in vanilla raids.
- **Stars:** every wave creature has **at least 1 star** (user, 2026-10-29), and up to 2 (design: tier 1 maps). The second star rolls with the game's own level-up chance.
- **Maddened:** red, damage ×1.2 (the game's own Dvergr power buff), speed +15% (the Tonic of Ratatosk), never flee, ~~and treat every creature and player as an enemy, so they fight each other too~~. **Since 2026-11-28 (Krogon's decisions 20–22 on `claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`; built, not play-tested): they fight players and players' tamed creatures only**, never each other, a corrupted boss, its summons or normal creatures, and normal creatures leave them alone (they act as vanilla towards players). Creatures near the heart still turn maddened when the waves start (decision 21). **Charms don't repel them** (user).
- **End loot (changed by the personal-loot round, 2026-11-26, passed alone on 1.0.30, merged in PR #88: there is no chest any more; the heart spills its loot on the ground and it is divided between the players, see [Personal loot and loot beams](#personal-loot-and-loot-beams); the text below is how it worked until then, with "chest" now meaning "spill"):** destroying the heart left a chest next to the exit rip: one of that biome's own vanilla chests with its normal loot (weighted by how often each appears in the biome) plus **1–3 corruption shards**, and since 2026-11-23 (Krogon's decisions 18, 25, 32) **one map of the rip's biome**, always, and **one Magic/Rare gear roll per player near the rip** (as a kill's, at the rip multiplier), beside the orb rolls ([Currency orb drops](#currency-orb-drops)). If the chest is full, what doesn't fit drops beside it. Destroying any rip heart also sets a world key (`corruption_rip_cleared`) that switches on creatures' map drops. Since 2026-11-26 the same loot spills around the heart on the heart owner's game: each player near the heart (100 m) keeps their own orb and Magic/Rare rolls, and the shared part (the biome chest's vanilla loot, the 1–3 shards, the map) is dealt between them. Krogon (decision 6): "If an odd number rolls, someone may miss out and that’s fine." The exit rip is unchanged.
- **Shards from rip creatures:** twice the normal starred chances (1-star 4%, 2-star 10%).

**How to test:** `spawn CorruptionMap_Meadows`, use it, go through: "The ground is shaking" (Eikthyr's army message), red boars and necks attack in waves; destroy the heart: exit rip plus a Meadows chest with shards. (Since 2026-11-26 there is no chest: the loot spills around the heart.)

**Data table:** [`Corruption/Data/rip_loot.csv`](Corruption/Data/rip_loot.csv): chests per biome and their weights, worked out from the 1.0.20 dump (each location's chest matched to its chest prefab by drop list). Compiled into the DLL. `dotnet test` checks every chest is a real container and every biome has one, and that every essence boss has an army raid.

**Config settings** (admin-only, `5 - Rips`):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `MinStars` | `1` | Fewest stars a rip wave creature has (user, 2026-10-29: waves weren't challenging enough). 0 = as vanilla rolls them |
| `MaxStars` | `2` | Most stars a rip creature can roll (design tier 1) |
| `WaveMultiplier` | `2` | Each raid entry's most-alive-at-once ×2 and spawn interval ÷2 in rips (user, 2026-10-29). 1 = the vanilla raid. **Since 2026-11-27 only used if `Corruption/Data/map_tiers.csv` fails to load** (the tier table sets the waves; tier 1 there is this ×2) |
| `ChestShardsMin` / `ChestShardsMax` | `1` / `3` | Shards in the rip chest (user); since 2026-11-26 the shards the heart spills (same keys). **Since 2026-11-27 only used if `map_tiers.csv` fails to load** (the tier table sets the shards: 1–4 at tier 1 up to 6–14 at tier 6) |
| `KillShardMultiplier` | `2` | Multiplies the starred shard chances for rip creatures (user) |

**Known issues:**
- **Unverified suspicion (review, 2026-11-07): limits after the heart changes hands.** The game that owns the rip heart counts the wave creatures it spawned itself, plus those loaded on it. If the heart passes to another player's game (its first owner walks away) while survivors are far from that player, the new owner may count too few and spawn past the limit. Not reproduced; nothing changed in the counting. Two log lines help check it with three players: `Rip waves: this game now runs the waves of rip …` when a game takes over, and `Rip waves: <creature> alive (counted here) X of Y; spawning Z` before each spawn (steps in `docs/playtests/claude-admiring-babbage-rk1w8d.md`).
- Untested in-game (103 tests pass). **Play-test 2026-10-29 didn't run step B:** no waves, no chest and no errors, because the build came from `master`, which doesn't have step B yet (it's on `claude/rips-bosses`). The log now names the build's branch and commit, so the next test can check.
- **Play-test 2026-10-29, right build:** waves start with Eikthyr's army message, creatures spawn, a 2-star dropped a shard. On a later run, destroying the heart opened the exit and left a Meadows chest with corruption shards (the log shows each step); on the first run the user saw no chest, not reproduced since.
- **Fixed 2026-10-29:** maddened creatures got **no damage or speed bonus**: the vanilla effects they copy were read at startup, before the game makes them available (log: "SE_Dvergr_buff not found"). They're now read in the world, the first time a creature is maddened, and the log says what was copied (`Maddened: damage ×1.2 …`). To re-test.
- **Play-test 2026-10-29 (build 65e6271): passed.** Maddened bonuses read correctly (log: `Maddened: damage ×1.2 (from SE_Dvergr_buff), speed +15% (from Potion_hasty)`); charms don't stop rip creatures; Meadows and Swamp rips each left a chest with shards. **Waves felt too slow** (Meadows: at most 4 alive, one every 20 s): rips now use `WaveMultiplier` 2 (user). Whether maddened creatures fight each other couldn't be judged yet. The user wants the heart moved away from the landing spot and found with a corrupted wishbone (to design).
- A `Failed to find rpc method 327122920` warning when first opening the chest is the game's own: vanilla chests call `discovered` but register `RPC_Discovered`. Harmless, happens with every vanilla treasure chest.
- **Multiplayer fixes 2026-11-01 (untested):** wave limits count creatures far from the heart's owner too; creatures maddened where they stand turn red for every player (message `Corruption_Maddened`).
- Maddened creatures stay after the heart is destroyed (the waves stop; the ones alive keep fighting). Vanilla raid creatures despawn when the raid ends; rips have no such end yet. **Changed 2026-11-27 (built, untested): for rips opened with this build, the wave creatures carry the rip's closing time and, once it has passed, become the game's "event creatures" and leave or despawn like a vanilla raid's when nobody fights them.** They leave once the rip has closed, not when the heart dies; creatures of rips opened before this build, and unstamped maddened creatures (a corrupted boss's summons), stay as before.
- **Play-test 2026-10-29:** the busier waves (`WaveMultiplier` 2) passed, and maddened creatures were seen fighting each other, as the design says.
- **Play-test 2026-10-29, after the busier waves:** still not challenging enough (user: too few or too weak). Wave creatures now have at least 1 star (`MinStars` 1). **Re-tested 2026-10-29: "definitely more challenging"** (user). With every creature starred, 1-star shard drops (4% in rips) apply to all of them, so rips give more shards, and 2 stars come one level-up roll sooner (10% instead of about 1%).
- Map tiers (more waves, more stars) come with phase 5. **Built 2026-11-27, untested: see [Map tiers and affixes](#map-tiers-and-affixes).**

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Character.Awake` | Postfix | Restores maddened behaviour on creatures loaded with the flag. *Since 2026-11-27 also remembers a loaded wave creature for the count (`BossArmies.Remember`)* |
| `Game.Start` | Postfix | Registers `Corruption_Maddened`: tells every game to show a creature maddened in place |
| `BaseAI.IsEnemy(Character, Character)` | Postfix, last priority | **Changed 2026-11-28 (decisions 20–22, built, not play-tested):** a corrupted creature (maddened, a corrupted boss or its summons) is an enemy of players and tamed creatures only, both ways; two creatures that aren't corrupted keep the game's answer (`CorruptedTargeting`, unit-tested). Runs after the talisman patch so charms don't repel them. ~~Maddened creatures are everyone's enemy; corrupted boss summons are enemies of players only~~ |
| `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList` | Postfix (the existing shard one) | Double starred shard chances for rip creatures |

New status effect `SE_CorruptionMaddened` (built from `SE_Dvergr_buff` and `Potion_hasty` at startup). The rip heart gets a wave spawner; the chest is the vanilla chest prefab itself.
