## Map tiers and affixes

*Corruption plugin, design phase 5, ROADMAP R1 and R7, issues [#31](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/31) and [#84](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/84). Branch `claude/festive-gates-79y1yr`, Krogon's decisions of 2026-11-26 (handoff `docs/handoffs/claude-festive-gates-79y1yr.md`, decisions 1–13). Design: `docs/corruption-design.md` → Map affixes and tiers. **Status: tested in-game alone and merged into `master` in PR #91 (Corruption 0.11.0)** (Krogon, single player, Valheim 1.0.31, 2026-11-27, three play-tests, see Play-test results below; two players untested; 729 tests pass; Corruption builds against the stand-in game files). The game's code was read in 1.0.14; every Corruption patch applied on 1.0.31.* **Changed 2026-11-27 (branch `claude/kind-einstein-8sn8e7`, issue [#90](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/90), ROADMAP R1b): the four affixes below are replaced by 17 and a map rolls a value for each; built, 756 tests pass, NOT play-tested.** The tier, orb, Forge, fallback and 96 m parts of this section stay as tested.*

**What it does in-game:**
- **Six tiers; a map's tier is its rarity and affix count** (decision 6). Krogon: "This is where we make a design change: Tier 1 is a white item / Tier 2 becomes a magic map with 1 affix / Tier 3 magic map with 2 affixes / Tier 4 rare map with 3 affixes / Tier 5 rare map with 4 affixes / New tier 6 corrupted map via a shard on top of a tier 5 map that already has 4 affixes". Tier 1 white (no affixes), 2 Magic with 1 affix, 3 Magic with 2, 4 Rare with 3, 5 Rare with 4, 6 a tier 5 map corrupted with a shard (locked). A map from before this build has no tier and counts as tier 1.
- **What a tier changes** (decision 6e; every number is in [`Corruption/Data/map_tiers.csv`](Corruption/Data/map_tiers.csv), tunable without touching the code, compiled into the DLL so rebuild after editing): the waves (most alive at once and spawn speed) ×2 / 2.5 / 3 / 3.5 / 4 / 4.5 (tier 1 is the old `WaveMultiplier` 2); guaranteed 2-stars none / 1 in 4 / 1 in 2 / 3 in 4 / all / all (a 25% chance per creature at tier 2, and so on; the rest roll as before, at least 1 star; 3-stars wait for the elite design, [#39](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/39)); the heart's shards 1–4 / 2–6 / 3–8 / 4–10 / 5–12 / 6–14, dealt one at a time among the players within 100 m (decision 4A, as decision 6 of the personal-loot round); the drop rates, the `rip_tier_1` … `rip_tier_6` rows of [`drop_modifiers.csv`](Corruption/Data/drop_modifiers.csv), ×1.25 / 1.5 / 1.75 / 2 / 2.25 / 2.5 for Magic, Rare, unique, orbs and creature map drops (decision 4b: "Tier 1 - everything x1.25 / Tier 2 - everything x1.5 / Tier 3 - everything 1.75 / Tier 4 - everything x2 / And so on"); each player's gear roll at the heart: by chance / at least Magic / Magic / Rare / Rare / Rare.
- **17 affixes with rolled values** *(Changed 2026-11-27, branch `claude/kind-einstein-8sn8e7`, #90; **tested alone 2026-11-28** (Valheim 1.0.31, all 30 steps), merged in PR #95 (Corruption 0.12.0, Orlog 0.10.0); Krogon's decisions 1–18 of that day in `docs/handoffs/claude-kind-einstein-8sn8e7.md`)*. ~~**Four affixes:** Swarming (waves ×2 again), Hardened (30% less damage taken), Rabid (maddened bonuses doubled), Smouldering (25% of a hit as fire); every affix danger only, decision 8b of the rips round: "A, every affix danger only. I must have misunderstood decision 7."~~ Krogon on the old four: "Remove each one. Use new affix pool I provided." And on 8b: "overwrite decision 8b, affixes can now provide rewards too" (Glorious is the first reward affix; the tier still pays too). The pool is Krogon's list; the table is [`Corruption/Data/map_affixes.csv`](Corruption/Data/map_affixes.csv) (id, status, name, danger text, weight, effect, min, max, group, notes). **The names are Claude's proposals** (Krogon let them stand; play-tested with them) (`docs/images/map_ui_tier5.png`, `map_ui_tiers.png`, redrawn). All weights are 100 (equally likely, Claude's choice carried over). This round builds the creature, player and wave affixes (decision 6: "Split rounds, agreed"); the loot affixes come next round and the corrupted-only ones after that; the weather ones still wait for `co_dump` ([#86](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/86)).
  - **Rolled values** (decision 3: "Yes that's why I provided the ranges, so that it can roll."): each map rolls a whole number inside the affix's range, kept as `id:value;id:value` in the map's custom data, sent in the rip request (the server clamps every value to the range; a NaN reads as the lowest) and stamped on the heart and on every creature. The text shows the rolled number. **The Divine Orb now rerolls a map's ranged values** (replaces the rips round's 6b, "Divine does nothing"); it is refused when no affix on the map has a range (all fixed ones), and a corrupted map stays locked. **Old maps are left as they are** (decision 14, 2026-11-27; Krogon: "Since everything is still in alpha essentially and we're not playing for real, it's fine. Leave them be as is and I'll delete whatever copies I have left."): there is no migration. A map that still carries a removed affix (Swarming, Hardened, Rabid, Smouldering) can't be worked at the Forge ("This item's affixes can't be read"), and an old corrupted map still shows tier 6 but opens a lower tier. Built; not part of the play-test (Krogon dropped the old-maps step).
  - **At most 4 affixes** (decision 4: "A map can only hold more then 4 affixes via means of corrupting a 5th affix on"). Claude's reading: the 5th is the corrupted-only affix of the corrupted round, so a tier 6 map still has 4 affixes until then.
  - **Groups** (decision 5: "Sure, create those rules. And yes, immune means takes 95% reduced damage."): never **Ironskin with Warded** (group `immunity`), never **Sturdy with Mighty** (group `health`). The orbs and `co_map`'s random pick respect them, and the server drops a second affix of a group (and an affix that is not built yet, status `later`) from a rip request. Affixes switched off by `DisabledMapAffixes` are kept (see below).
  - **"In the rip"** means within 96 m of a live rip's heart (decision 8: "Sure use the 96m"), the waves' range. It applies to the three player affixes.
  - **The wave and reward affixes:**
    - **Glorious** (50–100): enemies grant that much more experience. Sets the creature's ZDO float `corruption_rip_xp`; Orlog's kill XP (`Orlog/Content/KillXp.cs`) multiplies by it.
    - **Teeming** (fixed 50): the tier's waves ×1.5 (the old Swarming's job, a smaller factor).
  - **Creature damage:**
    - **Brutal** (20–100): enemies deal that much more damage.
    - **Ruthless** (10–50): each hit has that percent chance to deal double damage; one roll per hit.
    - **Venomous**, **Searing**, **Stormcalled** (25–75 each): each enemy hit adds that percent of its blunt, slash and pierce damage as poison, fire or lightning. Poison's length is set by the game from the size of the hit (so the text names no length); burning lasts 5 s (the game's own). All three, and Brutal and Ruthless, run in one `Character.Damage` prefix on the game that builds the hit, and skip damage-over-time hits so burning and poison never feed themselves.
  - **Creature toughness:**
    - **Ironskin** (95) and **Warded** (95): enemies take 95% less physical (blunt, slash, pierce) or elemental (fire, frost, lightning, poison; spirit is untouched) damage. "Immune" means 95% less, Krogon's decision 5. Applied on the victim's owner in `Character.RPC_Damage`.
    - **Sturdy** (25–50) and **Mighty** (50–100): enemies have that much more health (max health = base × level × factor). A creature at full health stays at full health when its maximum changes.
    - **Unyielding:** enemies can't be staggered. Side effects: a perfect parry no longer staggers them, no guard break on creatures that block, no adrenaline gained from staggering them, Orlog's Wind Lance doesn't stagger them, and the ×2 damage against staggered creatures never applies.
  - **Creature speed:**
    - **Fleet** (fixed 25): enemies move 25% faster. It adds like a speed status effect, so Fleet's +25% with the maddened bonus's +15% makes +40%. Not applied while frozen or stunned (speed 0). Flying creatures are covered too (`Character.UpdateFlying`).
    - **Frenzied** (25–100): enemies attack that much faster: the animator speed (on the owner only), a shorter wait between attacks (on the creature's own copy of the item, never the shared `m_shared`) and the game's "time since last attack".
  - **Player affixes, within 96 m of a live heart** (checked on each player's own game, every 0.5 s, by a `Player.UpdateStats` postfix, which adds the status effect "Rip curse", `SE_CorruptionRipCurse`, using the game's Poison icon; its tooltip lists the active lines):
    - **Withering** (fixed 5): you lose 5 health a second. True damage, like the game's own health tick: armour doesn't reduce it.
    - **Exhausting** (fixed 5): you lose 5 stamina a second without resetting the regen delay (the game's base stamina regen is also 5 a second).
    - **Exposed** (25–50): you have that much less resistance to fire, frost, lightning and poison. It is subtracted from Corruption's gear resistances after the 75% cap; below 0 means more damage taken. Vanilla resistances (meads and so on) are untouched. Done in the existing `LootResistances` prefix.
  - **The loot affixes** *(new 2026-11-28, branch `claude/nifty-hypatia-afm8ez`, [#96](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/96), ROADMAP R1c; **tested alone 2026-11-28** on Valheim 1.0.31, two players untested; Krogon's decisions 1–9 in `docs/handoffs/claude-nifty-hypatia-afm8ez.md`)*. Krogon's seven drop affixes; each is +100%, a ×2 on its drop chances and never a guarantee (decision 7 of the overhaul, his "+10%" lines read as +100%). All are rewards, listed under "What you'll earn", and as likely to roll as any other affix. Names and copy are Claude's proposals, which Krogon approved (decision 9). Rules: `Corruption/Rules/MapLootRules.cs` (pure, tested in `MapLootRulesTests.cs`).
    - **Bountiful:** "Enemies are 100% more likely to drop gear, orbs and maps." Only Corruption's own drops, i.e. magic, rare and unique gear rolls, every orb and the map roll (decision 3: "Our drops only"). The game's own creature drops are untouched.
    - **Glittering:** "Enemies are 100% more likely to drop orbs." Every orb's chance ×2; gear and maps unchanged.
    - **Bulwarked, Mailed, Honed, Fletched:** "Gear drops are 100% more likely to be shields / helmets and armour / melee weapons / ranged weapons." They **tilt the pick** (decision 5: "Tilt the pick"): the number of gear drops stays the same, but when gear drops, items of that kind weigh ×2 among the biome's bases, so other gear drops a little less often. Shields are the `shield` class of `loot_bases.csv`. Armour is helmet, chest, legs and cape, including backpacks (capes in the table) but not jewellery (decision 6). Ranged is bows, crossbows and magic staffs, by the game item's skill (`Bows`, `Crossbows`, `ElementalMagic`, `BloodMagic`), and every other weapon, pickaxes included, is melee (decision 7).
    - **Runic** (more runes) is a `later` row and never rolls until runes exist (decision 2).
    - **How they combine** (decision 4: "Multiply"): with the tier's and the stars' multipliers and with each other. Tier 3 orbs with Bountiful are 1.75 × 2 = ×3.5, and with Glittering too ×7.
    - **Where:** kills of the rip's stamped creatures (each player's own rolls, read from the creature's stamp in the existing `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList` postfix) **and the heart's spill** (decision 8: "Kills and the spill"; read from the heart's stamp in `RipLoot.Spill`, which logs the factors). No new Harmony patch.
    - **Testing:** `co_riploot [rolls] [tier] [affix ...]` rolls a rip creature's kill loot many times in the biome you stand in and prints the gear, orbs, map chance and the kinds of gear (e.g. `co_riploot 10000 3` against `co_riploot 10000 3 bountiful`). It drops nothing.
  - **Switching an affix off** (built 2026-11-27 on `claude/kind-einstein-8sn8e7`, **tested alone 2026-11-28**, merged in PR #95; Krogon: "make the option to disable some affixes from being able to roll - until we catch up and create the mechanics in the future that allow these affixes to function as intended"): the admin setting `[5 - Rips] DisabledMapAffixes` (server-synced; ids from `map_affixes.csv`, comma-separated, blank by default) keeps the listed affixes out of every roll (the orbs at the Forge tab and `co_map`'s random pick). Maps that already carry one keep it and it still acts (Claude's choice). Unknown ids are logged as a warning and ignored. An affix whose mechanic isn't built yet stays a `later` row, which never rolls either. No Harmony patch.
  - **Frostbitten, Drowned and Choking** (the weather ones) are rows with status `later`: they never roll. *Changed 2026-11-28 (`claude/nifty-hypatia-afm8ez`, decision 10): off the priority list; Krogon: "i dont think we will build them. but do not delete them entirely, just disable." They stay as `later` rows, and `co_dump`'s weather data ([#86](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/86)) is no longer needed for them.*
  - **Corrupted-only affixes: the 5th affix of a corrupted map** *(new 2026-11-28, branch `claude/nifty-hypatia-afm8ez`, [#97](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/97); **tested alone 2026-11-28** on Valheim 1.0.31, two players untested; decisions 11–15 in `docs/handoffs/claude-nifty-hypatia-afm8ez.md`)*. A row with `pool` = `corrupted` in `map_affixes.csv` is never rolled by an orb. The shard adds one at random (weighted, value rolled) when it corrupts a full tier 5 map, so the tier 6 map has 5 affixes (the rips round's decision 6d: "once corrupted-only affixes exist, every corrupted map gets one"). With none rollable (all switched off) the shard corrupts as before, with 4. The server keeps one corrupted-only affix, and only on a corrupted map with 4 normal ones (`MapData.Sanitize`); it doesn't count towards the 4. Its line is the last under "What you'll face", in the corrupted red (decision 15), on the tooltip, the Forge and the rip's hover. Maps corrupted before this build keep their 4 (overhaul decision 14: no migration). `co_map <biome> 6` adds one to a random tier 6 map; explicit ids are used as given.
    - **Thorns** (5–25, decision 12: "5-25%"; Krogon's corrupted list: "Monsters have thorns."; picked by Claude as the easiest, decision 11): "Enemies return {value}% of the damage you deal to them." Every hit a player deals to a stamped creature counts: melee, arrows, staffs, Orlog skills (decision 13: "Every hit"), but not damage over time. The share is of what the hit really took off the creature's health, so it counts after armour, resistances, Ironskin and Warded; burning and poison are applied later as damage over time and don't count. A killing blow counts what the creature had left. It comes back as a blunt hit that armour and blocking reduce (decision 14: "Yes, like a normal hit"). **Claude's choices:** the returned hit has no attacker, so it isn't raised by the creature's Brutal or the game's per-player enemy scaling and adds no Searing and the like; it has no stagger (no stun-lock) and no push. A parry of it still counts as a parry. **Known issue (balance, open):** armour cuts the small returned hits to almost nothing; Krogon saw 0.3–0.7 damage per iron-axe hit and wrote "may need to buff thorns". Done on the creature's owner (`Character.RPC_Damage` prefix and postfix), and the hit is sent to the player's own game. With `devcommands` on, each return prints `Thorns: your hit took N health, M comes back (P%, before your armour).` in the console and the log.
    - **Rimeburst** *(new 2026-11-28, branch `claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`, #97; **tested alone 2026-11-28** on 1.0.31, two players untested; Krogon: the damage feels low, the slow too weak, the look to improve later; decisions 4–10 in `docs/handoffs/claude-wizardly-wozniak-4aix76.md`)* (50–100, decision 7; Krogon's list: "Monsters trigger an explosion of Ice on death."): "Enemies explode in ice on death." (decision 10: the short line, no number; the rolled share still applies). When a stamped rip creature dies, its owner works out the frost: the map's share of the creature's own strongest hit (decision 4: its strongest weapon's fighting damage, without chop and pickaxe, times its attack's multiplier, times the game's star factor 1 + 0.5 per star, times Brutal) and tells every game. Each game shows a **1.5 s warning** (decisions 5 and 8): a pale blue ring at the **3 m** radius (decision 6, Freezing Arrow's 3.3 yards) that pulses faster, a growing light and the frost arrow's hit; then the **burst** (the Staff of Frost's hit effect). Preview: `docs/images/rimeburst_preview.png`. At the burst each game checks its own player: inside the ring it takes the frost as a hit with no attacker (armour, frost resistance, blocking and dodging reduce it, the game's frost slows it; no stagger, no push; Claude's choices). **Players only** (decision 9): enemies are never hit, so bursts don't chain. Every death of a stamped creature counts, whoever killed it (the guardian boss too). Log: `Rimeburst: <creature> (level N) bursts in 1.5 s for X frost (P% of its hit).` and `Rimeburst: caught in a burst of X frost`.
    - **Guarded** *(new 2026-11-28, same branch; **tested alone 2026-11-28** on 1.0.31 (Eikthyr, and the optional stronger bosses `cg-16`), two players untested; decisions 11–18)* (no value; Krogon's list: "A corrupted boss is Guarding the Heart."): "A corrupted boss guards the heart." When the waves start (a player within 96 m, decision 15), the heart's owner spawns **the biome's own boss** (decision 11: the boss whose essence makes the map; Kall starts at his first phase) 6 m from the heart on the side away from the players (Claude's choice), corrupted (red, 1.5× damage, 0.7× damage taken, **its summons**, decision 16) and stamped with the rip's affixes (decision 17), hunting the players (decision 12). **The heart takes no damage while it lives** (decision 13; its hover adds "Guarded: kill the corrupted boss first"). Its rewards are a corrupted boss's (decision 14: vanilla drops, 2–3 essences, 3–5 shards, each player's boss shard and orb) plus the rip's kill loot by tier. Kall's next phases guard the same heart with the same stamp. Once the rip closes it leaves like the other rip creatures (the game's event-creature despawn when nobody is near). Once per rip. **Not known until play-tested:** whether the Queen and Kall behave outside their arenas.
    - **Testing any creature affix alone** *(new 2026-11-28, decision 16, after Krogon's la-18 note that Thorns' damage was hard to see)*: `co_stamp [tier] <affix[:value] ...>` gives the creature you look at (or the nearest within 10 m) a rip's tier and affixes, as if a rip had spawned it; your game takes it over first. `co_stamp clear` removes it. Meant for the game's own training dummy (`spawn TrainingDummy`, 40,000 health, or the buildable T.W.I.G., 2,500 health): e.g. `co_stamp thorns:25`.
- **Making maps at the Riftforge's Forge tab, with the orbs exactly as with gear** (decision 6b: "A"). Transmutation white to tier 2, Augmentation tier 2 to 3, Alchemy or Chance (always Rare: no unique map exists) white to tier 4 (3 affixes), Regal tier 3 to 4, Exalted tier 4 to 5, Annulment removes one random affix so the map drops a tier (a Magic map with 1 affix becomes white), the Mirror of Kalandra copies a map (the copy of a corrupted map stays corrupted and locked). ~~Divine is refused ("Map affixes have no values to reroll.")~~ *(changed 2026-11-27, built, tested alone 2026-11-28: the Divine Orb rerolls the map's ranged values, and is refused only when no affix on the map has a range; a corrupted map stays locked)*, the Jeweller's Orb is refused ("Maps have no sockets."), Affix Splinters and Shatter are refused on maps. A map has no prefix or suffix sides: any different affixes, up to the tier's count. ~~**The shard corrupts a full tier 5 map into tier 6 and never breaks it**~~ **The shard corrupts a map of any tier and never breaks it** (*changed 2026-11-28, decisions 27–29 on `claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`, built, not play-tested*: it adds a corrupted-only affix and the map goes up one tier, tier 5 to 6 as before; every corrupted map is "Corrupted {biome} map" in red; still locked; the server reads a requested tier above the sent affixes' tier as corrupted) (decision 6c: "B. We should make corrupted only affixes that add fun and danger"); there is **no confirmation pop-up** (decision 14, 2026-11-27: Krogon, "The pop-up window on using a shard on a map is not required. lets remove it entirely. Players should know what a corruption shard does by now."): one click makes a full tier 5 map tier 6, with no explanation first (gear still asks first; **passed** in Krogon's final play-test, `mtf-1`); no orb changes a corrupted map afterwards, and a shard is refused on a map below tier 5. Maps go into the forge from the bag or the Maps tab: drag, or right-click while the Forge tab is open (right-clicking a map there no longer opens a rip). The forge's pictures were approved by Krogon first (decision 13: "Preview pictures look good. Approved."); the corrupt-map picture `docs/images/forge_map_corrupt.png` is superseded by decision 14 and kept only as the record.
- **Names and display** (decision 11: "b, yes yes yes"): the name stays "{biome} map" in the rarity colour (white, Magic blue, Rare yellow); a tier 6 map is "Corrupted {biome} map" in red. The tier shows as a small number in the corner of the map's icon, where the game shows an item's quality, in the rarity colour, in every grid. **Tooltip and Forge, redesigned 2026-11-27 (Krogon's decision 15, approved mockup `docs/images/map_ui_approved_mockup.png`; tested in game alone the same day, `mtg-5` … `mtg-8`; preview `docs/images/map_ui_tier5.png` and `map_ui_tiers.png`, drawn by `tools/mockup_map_ui.py` from the code's own text):** both views share one summary built in `MapText` (pure, unit-tested): the usage line "Opens a rip into the {biome}. The map crumbles once used.", then **What you'll face** (the two-star line: "Every enemy spawned in a wave has two stars." at 100%, "Each enemy spawned in a wave has at least a N% chance of two stars." in between, "Enemies spawned in a wave may have two stars by chance." at 0; then one bullet per danger affix on the map, in `map_affixes.csv` order: **the affix's line alone, with its rolled number, in the rarity colour, and no name** (decision 10, built 2026-11-27, tested alone 2026-11-28; Krogon: "The names of affixes do not need to be shown on the tooltip, it uses up already limited ui space."; the names stay in the table for logs and `co_rip`); the old "Name — danger" bullets were the tested layout) and **What you'll earn** ("Destroy the heart for 5–12 Corruption Shards total.", the gear floor "Each eligible player gets Rare/Magic gear or better." or, at tier 1, "The heart may also drop gear.", and "Corruption loot is 2.25× as likely to drop during the rip."; **reward affixes (Glorious now) are listed here too, after those lines**, decision 16: "Move to earn (Recommended)", the `section` column of `map_affixes.csv`, built, tested alone 2026-11-28). Every number comes from `map_tiers.csv` and `drop_modifiers.csv`; the affix texts are `map_affixes.csv`'s `danger` column (Krogon's copy). Headings warm gold `#F8BC42` (from the mockup), text cream `#EDE3CF`, the existing gear bullets. The **tooltip** reads: title (name in rarity colour), the coloured "Tier 5 · Rare map" line, the summary, the Corrupted/Mirrored marks centred, and the game's own lines (weight) dim at the bottom. In the **Forge tab** the left column keeps the icon with its tier number, shows the tier line once under it (and the marks), and the Remove button; the right column has the name, the summary (the last orb's added affixes highlighted) and the crafting result in a band at the bottom, sized to the message; **since 2026-11-28 (decision 24 on `claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`, built, not play-tested) there is no crafting result text; the highlight shows the change;** **Shatter is hidden while a map is in the forge**; the description text shrinks (down to 70%) rather than clip, or scrolls if the game's panel scrolls. "—", "×" and "’" fall back to "-", "x" and "'" if the game's font lacks them (checked once per font). Gear is unchanged. The rip's entry, exit and heart show "Tier N · affixes" on hover, so the whole group sees what it walks into. **Changed 2026-11-27 (decision 15, "Tier and the lines"; built, tested alone 2026-11-28):** the portal's and heart's hover show the tier and each affix's line with its number, like the tooltip, instead of the names.
- **Every dropped map is tier 1, with no affixes** (decision 5: "Option a"): creature map drops and the one map the heart spills. Raising a map costs orbs at the Riftforge.
- **Rips follow the map.** Using a map sends its tier and affixes with the rip request (after the candidate spots); the server clamps the tier to the table, drops unknown affixes (and, since 2026-11-27, clamps each value to its range, reads NaN as the lowest, and drops a not-yet-built `later` affix and a second affix of a group), derives the tier from the affix count (corrupted only with a full set) and echoes them. The opener's game uses up the exact map used (or an identical one) and stamps the tier and affixes on the rip's entry and the heart (object data `corruption_rip_tier`, `corruption_rip_affixes`); the exit copies them. The heart's owner runs the waves by the heart's stamp; every wave creature (and every creature maddened in place) is stamped too and its affixes act from its own stamp (since 2026-11-27 the stamp is `id:value;...` and each effect runs where the game needs it: the hit's builder, the victim's owner, the creature's owner; see the patch table). A creature keeps its stamp after a reload. Kill loot follows the creature's tier; an unstamped maddened creature (a corrupted boss's summon, a rip opened before this build) counts as tier 1 as before. The heart spills by its tier. A map that gets no answer in 15 seconds says "The map falls silent. Try again." A tiered map used on a server that has no tiers (an older build) is kept, with "The server's Corruption mod is older than yours: this map's tier can't open there. Update the server; the map is kept."
- **Wave spawns skip spots inside a player base** (the game's own raid rule, an `EffectArea` of type PlayerBase), and every game counts the wave creatures it has loaded, not only the ones it spawned itself (improves the count after the heart changes owner; creatures the new owner never loaded are still missed).

**Play-test results (2026-11-27, Krogon alone in single player, Valheim 1.0.31; build `claude/festive-gates-79y1yr f1bc90c`, with EquipmentAndQuickSlots 3.1.3, AdventureBackpacks 2.4.6, Jotunn 2.30.2; steps `mt-1` … `mt-21` of `docs/playtests/claude-festive-gates-79y1yr.md`):**
- **Confirmed in game:** every Forge tab and tooltip step (tier numbers on the icons, tooltips, every orb on maps, refusals, Annulment, Alchemy, the Mirror of Kalandra, gear still working, old maps counting as tier 1); and a tier 6 Meadows rip on unexplored land: the 96 m check ("Rip request: 29 candidate(s), 27 away from player-placed objects (96 m); checked 2 placed object(s) near them of 223274 in 25 ms."), the waves (x9, 100% guaranteed 2-stars), Rabid ("Maddened (Rabid, factor 2): damage ×1.4, speed +30%."), and the heart's loot by tier (11 shards, 1 Rare gear item, 2 orbs and one white `CorruptionMap_Meadows`).
- **Every patch applied on 1.0.31:** the log has no "Failed to apply patch" line and no Harmony error from Corruption, so the eleven patch targets this round lists applied. The only Harmony warning is Orlog's known `ConnectPanel.m_root`, which Orlog handles. The rip request's game members worked (the scan ran).
- **Waiting for the final play-test:** the explored-land fallback ([#84](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/84)) and its timing (`Minimap.IsExplored` is not yet confirmed), the leftover creatures leaving, the tier 3 log line, "the exact map used is used up", no pop-up when a map is corrupted, and no red errors. Steps `mt-12` … `mt-20` were marked Pass, but his log shows only the one tier 6 rip, so the rest is not counted as tested; the white-map rip and the tier 3 and 4 rips are not in the log either.
- **Found:** `mt-5` had no mark, only the note that became decision 14 (no pop-up). `mt-21` failed: red Unity errors from the loot beams (see [Personal loot and loot beams](#personal-loot-and-loot-beams)), fixed the same day.

**Final play-test (2026-11-27, the same day, Valheim 1.0.31, build `claude/festive-gates-79y1yr f4d8580`; steps `mtf-1` … `mtf-6`):**
- **Passed:** no pop-up when a shard corrupts a map (`mtf-1`); the loot beams' smoke ("beams are fine, no red errors", `mtf-3`): no red error in the whole log. From the log: the fallback rip opened (`Rip request: 77 candidate(s) (explored-land fallback), 77 away from player-placed objects (96 m); checked 0 placed object(s) near them of 237778 in 38 ms.`), tier 3's `waves x6, 50% guaranteed 2-stars`, the heart by tier 3 (8 shards, a Magic gear item, a white map).
- **Failed, fixed:** the fallback rip opened on land he had never been to (`mtf-2`: "Saw the log but the rip opened into unexplored land where I havent been before."; `mtf-3`: "but spawned in explored territory", read as the same problem). Cause: the fallback dropped the "unexplored" requirement instead of asking for explored land, so it took any land of the biome (in a world with the biome fully explored that is the same thing, but not with `co_rip fallback on`, nor when the first search simply missed). Now the fallback takes **explored land only**, for the heart and the landing spot (`RipRules.ExplorationFits`, unit-tested; 730 tests).
- **Not in the log, so still to try:** the white-map rip back on unexplored land (`mtf-4`) and the leftovers leaving after the rip closes (`mtf-5`); both were marked Pass, but the log has one rip only and no settings reload (`OpenDays` was not changed in that game).

**Re-test (2026-11-27, the same day; steps `mtg-1` … `mtg-8`): passed.** Krogon: "Playtested. UI looks much better." All eight marked Pass: the fallback on explored land after its fix, unexplored land again, the leftovers leaving once the rip closed, no red errors, and the redesigned tooltip and Forge view (decision 15) at tiers 1, 3, 5 and 6 and at a smaller GUI scale. His saved log of that game (build `eb7ce0f`, Valheim 1.0.31, no errors): the fallback rip opened on explored land (`Rip request: 10 candidate(s) (explored-land fallback), 3 away from player-placed objects (96 m); checked 3 placed object(s) near them of 250075 in 20 ms.`, a tier 3 Meadows rip next to where his first play-test's rip had been), then, after the settings reload for `OpenDays`, a white-map rip on unexplored land (16 ms). The log doesn't record creatures leaving, so the leftovers stand on his mark.

### Explored-land fallback and the 96 m rule (#84)

- **96 m from everything a player placed, for every rip** (decision 12). Krogon: "96m, every rip, everything a player placed". Neither the heart nor the landing spot may be within 96 m (config `BaseDistance`, 0 = 96 m: the army raids' own `m_eventRange` and the waves' range) of anything a player placed: any object the game marks with a builder id (buildings, fires, portals, beds, wards, crops, saplings, carts, boats). The server checks it in one pass over all its objects, with a set lookup first, and logs the time: "Rip request: N candidate(s) …, M away … checked K placed object(s) near them of Z in X ms". Grown crops (a Plant's grown Pickables, e.g. Pickable_Barley) count as player-made; grown trees from saplings can't be told from wild ones and don't.
- **Unexplored land stays the first choice** (8000 samples, 30 candidates). Only when the opener's game finds none, it searches explored land only (16000 samples, 80 candidates; the heart and the landing spot both explored; until 2026-11-27's fix it took any land, see Final play-test above) and says "No unexplored {biome} is left: the rip opens on land you've explored, away from anything players built." If the server rejects every unexplored candidate, the player gets "Every spot of {biome} the map found is too close to something players built. Try again." and no fallback follows.
- Other texts: "The map finds no flat {biome} to open a rip on, explored or not. Try again." (nothing found at all) and "The map you used is gone. No rip opens." (the used map left the inventory during the wait).

**How to test (alone, with the console):** the cheats need devcommands.
- `co_map <biome> [tier] [affix ...]` puts a map of any tier and affixes in your inventory, e.g. `co_map BlackForest 6`, `co_map Meadows 3 brutal mighty:80 withering` (updated 2026-11-27: each affix is `id` or `id:value`; an unspecified value rolls; explicit ids may break the group rule, in which case the server drops the second of a group at the rip; the command lists the ids). `co_rip` now also prints the rolled values, and `RipWaves` logs them once per rip.
- `co_rip` shows the nearest rip object within 200 m: biome, tier, affixes, time left, the heart and its owner.
- `co_rip fallback on` (or `off`) skips the unexplored search on this game so the explored-land fallback can be tested without exploring a biome (not saved).
- At the Riftforge: put a map in the Forge tab and use orbs (orbs from drops, or `spawn` them). The steps are in `docs/playtests/claude-festive-gates-79y1yr.md` and the phone page; the two-player steps are in `docs/PLAYTEST.md` → Session 2, 2m.

**Data tables** (all compiled into the DLL, rebuild after editing; `dotnet test` validates them):
- [`map_tiers.csv`](Corruption/Data/map_tiers.csv): per tier its rarity, affix count, corrupted flag, waves, guaranteed 2-star percentage, heart shards, drop-rate row and the heart's gear floor, each with its source in the notes column.
- [`map_affixes.csv`](Corruption/Data/map_affixes.csv): the affixes, their status (`approved` or `later`), name, danger text with a `{value}` slot, weight, effect, the range (`min`, `max`; equal = fixed, empty = none) and the exclusion group. *(Changed 2026-11-27: the old per-affix number columns `wave_multiplier`, `damage_taken`, `maddened_factor`, `fire_share` are gone.)*
- [`drop_modifiers.csv`](Corruption/Data/drop_modifiers.csv): `rip_tier_1` … `rip_tier_6`, +0.25 per tier (the tier 2 and 3 rows changed from ×1.25 and ×1.5 to ×1.5 and ×1.75).

**Config changes:** `BaseDistance` now means 96 m from anything a player placed (0 = 96 m); `WaveMultiplier`, `ChestShardsMin` and `ChestShardsMax` keep their keys and defaults but are only used if `map_tiers.csv` fails to load; the per-player `WarnBeforeCorruptingMaps` that this round first added was removed again on 2026-11-27 (decision 14), so there is no new option.

**Console and texts:** the new texts are `corruption_rip_fallback`, `corruption_rip_map_gone`, `corruption_rip_old_server`, `corruption_rip_no_answer` (now used), and the reworded `corruption_rip_no_land` and `corruption_rip_only_bases` (`Texts.cs`).

**Known issues and limits:**
- **Tiers, orbs, Forge, fallback and the tooltip layout passed Krogon's solo play-tests** (the rips round's re-test, `mtg-1` … `mtg-8`; two players untested); the affix overhaul above passed Krogon's solo play-test on 2026-11-28 (`ma-1` … `ma-31`, `ma-6b`; two players untested; merged in PR #95). Reviewed in code by two Opus reviews (patches against the game's code and `docs/mod-compatibility.md`; multiplayer and the dedicated server) and the fixes made (2026-11-27): the new rip fields are read only when present; the server scan does one set lookup per object first; map use checks the item is in the inventory used; a map changed at the Forge during the 15-second wait isn't used up at its old tier; the icon's tier number is cached per item.
- **Not built:** the three weather affixes (next round), corrupted-only affixes (later), 3-star creatures ([#39](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/39)).
- **Affix overhaul, known limits (2026-11-27; the round passed alone on 2026-11-28, these limits were not tried one by one):** life steal on Ironskin and Warded maps counts the damage after their 95% cut (decision 11, built; "Lifesteal should steal the amount it damages for."); for all other armour and resistances life steal still counts the hit before them, and Krogon agreed to change that later (decision 13), [#93](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/93). Fleet adds its full bonus while swimming (vanilla speed effects add half). A creature already hurt when a rip starts keeps its health points while its maximum rises. Tier 6 still has 4 affixes until the corrupted round. The names are Claude's proposals; they show only in logs and the console (decision 10). Verified only by reading the code and by unit tests; nothing was run in the game.
- The server trusts the client's tier number, clamped to the table and derived from the affix count (friends only).
- The tier number uses the game's own small quality text; a mod that also writes it would fight ours.
- The Ashlands army's charred spawner stones spawn creatures that aren't stamped: no tier drop rates and no affixes on them (an older gap).
- After the heart changes owner, the new game may still undercount far survivors (the 2026-11-07 suspicion above): creatures it never loaded aren't counted, and a Teeming map makes the cap bigger (the old Swarming at tier 5 and 6 made up to 96 and 108 creatures in the Black Forest; Teeming's ×1.5 is smaller, all run by one game, about 70–80 bytes more per creature for the stamps). `Begin()` maddens only what the heart's owner has loaded.
- Two rips can overlap. The server scans every object once per map use (the log prints the time; if it passes about 200 ms, a sector search comes next).
- Mixed builds inside 0.10.0 were not turned away by Jötunn; the coordinator's bump to **0.11.0** at the merge (2026-11-27, PR into `master`) closes that: a 0.10.0 game can't join a 0.11.0 server.
- For the weather round (from the multiplayer review): each game works out its own weather, so each client must set it from data it can read; the heart may not be loaded at the landing spot (it depends on the server's simulation distance); after the heart dies only the exit carries the stamp; smoke damage must run on each player's own game. See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` → Game facts.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):** everything new acts only on creatures stamped by a rip or on map items; unstamped creatures and other items are untouched. *2026-11-28 (#96, #97, built, not play-tested):* the loot affixes need no new patch (they ride the existing `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList` postfix and `RipLoot.Spill`); Thorns adds a prefix and postfix on `Character.RPC_Damage` (class `MapAffixEffects.Thorns`, Normal priority, never skips; row below). *2026-11-28 (`claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`, #97, built, not play-tested):* Rimeburst adds a `Character.OnDeath` prefix and a `Game.Start` postfix (message registration); Guarded adds a `Destructible.RPC_Damage` prefix (rows below) and changes the bodies of existing patches only (`Character.Awake` boss watcher in `CorruptedBosses`, the corrupted-phase `Character.OnDeath` prefix and `Character.Awake` postfix in `CorruptedOfferings`).

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Character.Damage` | Prefix at `Priority.Low` (`MapAffixEffects`; replaces Smouldering's) | **Changed 2026-11-27, tested alone 2026-11-28.** On the game that builds the hit, for a stamped rip creature's hit: Brutal's damage, Ruthless's double damage (one roll per hit) and the Venomous, Searing and Stormcalled shares (a percent of the hit's blunt, slash and pierce added as poison, fire or lightning). Never skips the original; skips damage-over-time hits (burning, poison) so they never feed themselves. Runs after Orlog's crit (VeryHigh), Corruption's on-hit affixes (High) and life steal (Normal) |
| `Character.RPC_Damage` | Prefix (Normal; `MapAffixEffects`; replaces Hardened's) | **Changed 2026-11-27, tested alone 2026-11-28.** On the victim's owner: Ironskin or Warded cut physical (blunt, slash, pierce) or elemental (fire, frost, lightning, poison) damage by 95%; spirit untouched. Never skips the original, never touches players |
| `Character.RPC_Damage` | Prefix + postfix (Normal; `MapAffixEffects.Thorns`, a separate class) | **New 2026-11-28 (#97), built, not play-tested.** On a stamped creature's owner: the prefix notes its health when a player's (non damage-over-time) hit arrives; the postfix sends the Thorns share of the health it lost back to that player as a blunt hit (`Character.Damage` on the player; no attacker, no stagger, blockable). Never skips |
| `Character.OnDeath` | Prefix (Normal; `Rimeburst.Burst`, `CorruptedMapAffixes.cs`) | **New 2026-11-28 (#97), built, not play-tested.** On a dying stamped creature's owner: works out Rimeburst's frost and sends the burst (routed message `Corruption_Rimeburst` to every game). Reads only, never skips |
| `Game.Start` | Postfix (`Rimeburst.RegisterRpc`) | **New 2026-11-28.** Registers the `Corruption_Rimeburst` message |
| `Destructible.RPC_Damage` | Prefix at `Priority.First` (`RipGuardian.ProtectHeart`, `CorruptedMapAffixes.cs`) | **New 2026-11-28 (#97), built, not play-tested.** On the heart's owner: a Guarded heart whose guardian boss is alive (loaded there) skips the original, so the hit does nothing. Every other object, and a heart without Guarded or with its guardian dead, is untouched |
| `Character.SetupMaxHealth` | Prefix + postfix (`MapAffixEffects`) | Sturdy and Mighty: max health = base × level × factor; a creature at full health stays full |
| `SEMan.ApplyStatusEffectSpeedMods` | Prefix + postfix (`MapAffixEffects`) | Fleet: adds its bonus like a speed status effect (+25% and maddened's +15% make +40%); not while frozen or stunned at speed 0 |
| `Character.UpdateFlying` | Prefix + finalizer (`MapAffixEffects`) | Fleet for flying creatures; the finalizer restores the values even if the game's method throws |
| `CharacterAnimEvent.Speed` | Prefix (`MapAffixEffects`, a separate class from the player's Swift in `LootGathering.cs`) | Frenzied: animator speed, on the creature's owner only. Both classes patch the same method; each multiplies only its own targets |
| `CharacterAnimEvent.CustomFixedUpdate` | Postfix (`MapAffixEffects`) | Frenzied: keeps the animator's resting speed scaled (the clips set it through `Speed`, it rests at 1 otherwise) |
| `Attack.Start` | Postfix (`MapAffixEffects`) | Frenzied: a shorter wait between attacks, written to the creature's own item copy, never the shared `m_shared` |
| `Humanoid.GetTimeSinceLastAttack` | Postfix (`MapAffixEffects`) | Frenzied: the game's "time since last attack" runs faster for the stamped creature |
| `Character.AddStaggerDamage` | Prefix at `Priority.Last` (`MapAffixEffects`) | Unyielding: skips the original for a stamped creature (side effects listed under Unyielding above) |
| `Character.Stagger` | Prefix at `Priority.Last` (`MapAffixEffects`) | Unyielding: skips the original for a stamped creature |
| `Player.UpdateStats` | Postfix (`MapAffixPlayers`) | Each player's own game, every 0.5 s: if a live heart with Withering, Exhausting or Exposed is within 96 m, adds the status effect "Rip curse" (`SE_CorruptionRipCurse`, via `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`); its tick deals the 5 health and takes the 5 stamina a second |
| `Character.RPC_Damage` | The existing `LootResistances` prefix (`LootPlayerStats.cs`; changed) | Exposed subtracts its percent from Corruption's gear resistances after the 75% cap |
| `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui` | Postfix (default priority; `MapTierBadge.UpdateGui_Patch` in `MapForge.cs`) | Shows a map's tier number in the quality text of its cell and restores the colour elsewhere. Touches only the quality text of map cells |
| `ItemData.GetTooltip` | Postfix (the loot tooltip's existing one; changed) | A map gets its own tooltip branch first: since 2026-11-27 (decision 15) it replaces the game's text with tier line, summary (what you'll face / earn) and marks, and keeps the game's remaining lines (weight, other mods' lines) without the map's description, dim at the bottom |
| `InventoryGrid.CreateItemTooltip` | Postfix (changed) | A map's tooltip title in its rarity colour |
| `ItemDrop.GetHoverText` | Postfix (changed) | A dropped map's name in its rarity colour |
| `Humanoid.UseItem` | Prefix (changed; `Rips.UseMap`) | Passes the exact map used, checks it is in the inventory used, and sends its tier and affixes with the request |
| `InventoryGui.OnRightClickItem` | Prefix (changed; `RiftforgeWorkspace`) | With the Forge tab open, a right-clicked own map goes into the forge |
| `Player.UpdateTeleport` | Postfix (changed; `SnapAfterTeleport`) | Also shows the 15-second "The map falls silent. Try again." when a rip request gets no answer |
| `CharacterDrop.OnDeath` | Postfix (the existing loot one; changed) | The tier's drop rates follow the creature's stamp (`LootPatches.Rolls`) |
| `Character.Awake` | Postfix (the Maddened one; changed) | Also remembers a loaded wave creature for the count (`BossArmies.Remember`), so every game counts the wave creatures it has loaded |

**Removed 2026-11-27 (decision 1):** Hardened's `Character.RPC_Damage` prefix, Smouldering's `Character.Damage` prefix (replaced by the one above) and Rabid's status effect in `Maddened.cs`. Status effect added through `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`: the "Rip curse" (AdventureBackpacks prefixes that method but only acts for Wet and Cold; see `docs/mod-compatibility.md`).

Also: the pure rules (`MapTierTable`, `MapAffixTable`, `MapOrbRules`, `MapCorruptRules`, `MapText`, `RipTierRules`, `RipRules`) have no game dependencies and are unit-tested (`MapTierTests`, `MapTextTests`, `RipFallbackTests`). Game members relied on, to confirm on 1.0.30: `ZDOMan.m_objectsByID`, `ZDO.GetPosition`/`GetLong`/`GetInt`/`GetString`/`Set`, `ZDOVars.s_creator` (`Piece.SetCreator`), `ZPackage.GetPos`/`Size`, `Minimap.IsExplored`, `WorldGenerator.GetBiome`/`GetHeight`/`GetTerrainDelta`, `ZoneSystem.ZoneLocation.m_maxTerrainDelta`, `Character.Damage(HitData)`, `Character.RPC_Damage(long, HitData)`, `HitData.GetAttacker`/`ApplyModifier`/`m_hitType`/`m_damage`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`/`RemoveStatusEffect`/`HaveStatusEffect`, `SE_Stats.m_damageModifier`/`m_speedModifier`/`m_modifyAttackSkill`, `SE_Dvergr_buff`, `Potion_hasty`, `Character.SetLevel`, `SpawnSystem.GetLevelUpChance`, `Destructible.m_onDestroyed`, `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui`/`GetElement`/`m_quality`, `ItemData.GetTooltip`/`Clone`, `Inventory.ContainsItem`/`RemoveOneItem`/`AddItem`/`HaveEmptySlot`/`Changed`, `Humanoid.UseItem(Inventory, ItemData, bool)`, `ZoneSystem.m_vegetation`, `Plant.m_grownPrefabs`, `EffectArea.IsPointInsideArea`, `MonsterAI.SetEventCreature`/`IsEventCreature`, `ZDO.GetPrefab`.

**Conflicts:** see `docs/mod-compatibility.md`. None of EquipmentAndQuickSlots, AdventureBackpacks and PlantEasily patches `Character.Damage` or `RPC_Damage` (per the recorded lists); AdventureBackpacks' `GetTooltip` postfix (priority 200) is unaffected, as we only append.

## Rip heart search and corrupted wishbone

*Corruption plugin, after the step B play-test (user, 2026-10-29): "the heart is literally right next to the portal; the players need to go find it", guided by a corrupted wishbone.*

**What it does in-game:**
- **The heart is out of sight.** Players land 96–128 m from the rip heart, in a random direction. 96 m is the boss army raids' own range (every `army_*` raid in the 1.0.20 dump), so the landing spot is quiet and **the waves start as you close in on the heart**; 128 m is the user's choice. Heart and landing spot both pass the old checks (the map's biome, above water, flat, unexplored, away from bases).
- **Corrupted wishbone.** Crafted at the Riftforge from 1 Wishbone and 1 corruption shard (the design's rule for every corrupted item: the normal item plus 1 shard). Worn in the utility slot it works like the vanilla Wishbone, but points at **the heart of the rip you came through**: red-and-purple pings, every 5 s at the edge of its range and every 0.5 s on top of the heart (the vanilla Wishbone's own pacing and its near/medium/far pings), within 128 m of the heart (the farthest you can land from it). **Everyone nearby sees and hears the pings** (user).
- Your game remembers the heart when you go through the entry rip, in your character's saved data, so it still works after logging out inside the rip. It forgets it when the heart is destroyed, the rip closes, or you go home through the exit rip.
- Without the wishbone you can still find the heart: the waves start within 96 m of it.

**How to test:** `spawn Wishbone`, `spawn CorruptionShard`, craft the Corrupted wishbone at the Riftforge (or `spawn CorruptedWishbone`) and wear it. `spawn CorruptionMap_Meadows`, use it, go through: no heart in sight, no waves; red/purple pings, faster as you walk towards the heart; waves start on the way; destroy the heart, pings stop. A second player nearby sees your pings.

**Config settings:** none. The distances come from the game data and the user (`RipRules.HeartMinDistance` / `HeartMaxDistance`; a test checks every army raid's range still fits).

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-29 (single player): works.** The heart is out of sight on landing, the wishbone's pings lead to it. The pings looked **mostly purple** with some red; the user wants mostly red, so now only about a fifth of the colour range shades into purple (`PurpleShare` in `CorruptedWishbone.cs`). **Re-checked 2026-10-29: more red now, and the pings stop when the heart dies** (user).
- Not yet tested with a second player (whether they see the pings). The log says how the pings reach other players (`shared with other players by …`).
- It only finds rip hearts, not buried treasure; keep the vanilla Wishbone for that.
- Rips opened before this update still have the heart beside the landing spot.
- The landing spot still doesn't avoid locations (villages, altars), as before.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Game.Start` | Postfix | Registers the ping message (`Corruption_RipPing`) |

New item `CorruptedWishbone` (cloned from `Wishbone`, tinted). New status effect `SE_CorruptedWishbone` (class `SE_RipFinder`: the vanilla `SE_Finder` behaviour aimed at the remembered heart). New effect prefabs `corruption_<vanilla ping effect>` (tinted clones of the Wishbone's pings). The heart's position is stored on the entry rip (`corruption_rip_heart`) and in the player's custom data (`corruption_rip_heart`).

## Corrupted offerings

*Corruption plugin, design phase 6: summoning corrupted bosses. Which item each boss takes and how many come from the game data (`altars.csv`); the rules from the user (2026-10-28 and 2026-10-30).*

**What it does in-game:** each boss's vanilla offering has a corrupted version, crafted at the **Riftforge** and shown with its own icon (the vanilla icon recoloured dark red with a glowing crack, from the user's renders). How it's used follows the boss's own summoning:

| Boss | Corrupted item | Recipe | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| Eikthyr | Corrupted deer trophy | 2 Deer trophies + 1 shard | Offer it at the altar like the trophies: the one item is the whole offering |
| The Elder | Corrupted ancient seed | 3 Ancient seeds + 1 shard | Same |
| Bonemass | Corrupted withered bone | 10 Withered bones + 1 shard | Same |
| Moder | Corrupted dragon egg | 1 Dragon egg + 1 shard | Put one in **every** egg cup (3), then use the altar |
| Yagluth | Corrupted fuling totem | 1 Fuling totem + 1 shard | One on **every** totem stand (5) |
| The Queen | Corrupted sealbreaker | 1 Sealbreaker + 1 shard | Open her door with it: bosses in that location turn corrupted, now or when they appear |
| Fader | Corrupted bell | 1 Bell + 1 shard | One in **every** bell holder (3) |
| Kall Fimbulbringer | Corrupted malicious blood | 3 Malicious blood + 1 shard | Offer it at his altar; his later phases stay corrupted |

- At a stand altar, a **mix** of corrupted and normal items is refused ("Every offering here must be corrupted, or none."), so no corrupted item is used up on a normal fight.
- Pressing Use on an empty stand, or on the Queen's door, uses a **corrupted** item first if you carry one. Carry only normal ones for a normal fight.
- A corrupted item offered at the wrong altar gets the game's own "wrong offering" message.
- Corrupted items are only found in your **main inventory**, not in an AdventureBackpacks backpack (unlike normal keys, which AB lets you use from the backpack).
- **Who summons (review fix 2026-11-08; passed alone 2026-11-08 at Eikthyr and Moder, two players untested: Session 2, 2g):** like a normal offering, a corrupted one asks the game that runs the altar to summon; that game refuses while a boss is already on its way, finds the spawn point, and only then marks the summon corrupted and asks the offering player's game to take the one corrupted item. So offering anything during the spawn delay (a normal item, a wrong item, another corrupted one, from you or another player) changes nothing: one corrupted boss, one corrupted item taken. Before, a normal or wrong item offered during the delay turned the coming boss normal (after the corrupted item was taken), and a second player offering a corrupted item during the delay took over the altar and summoned a second boss. At a stand altar, the altar's game checks again that every stand holds a corrupted item.

**How to test:** `devcommands`, then `spawn CorruptedTrophyDeer` (or `CorruptedAncientSeed`, `CorruptedWitheredBone`, `CorruptedDragonEgg`, `CorruptedGoblinTotem`, `CorruptedDvergrKey`, `CorruptedBell`, `CorruptedHatefulBlood`), go to the boss's altar (`goto` or the map) and offer it. The boss arrives red and corrupted; the log says `Corrupted offering: Eikthyr summoned corrupted.`

**Data table:** [`Corruption/Data/offerings.csv`](Corruption/Data/offerings.csv): boss, vanilla item, how it's used (`altar`, `stand`, `door`), vanilla items per corrupted item, name and description. Compiled into the DLL. `dotnet test` checks every row against the game's altars (item, count, stand altars) and that each has its icon.

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-30 (single player):** Eikthyr (one corrupted trophy summons him corrupted), Moder (all egg cups corrupted; a mix refused) and the Queen (her door with the corrupted Sealbreaker) passed. **Bug, fixed:** the Ancient seed and Malicious blood icons were swapped (the renders were matched to the wrong items when saved). **Kall's phases stay corrupted** (tested 2026-10-30 with `spawn FrozenKing`, `co_corrupt`, `killall`: phases 2 and 3 came up red).
- Kall's phases: the next phase is corrupted when a corrupted phase dies and a boss named like its next phase (`FrozenKing_p2`, `_p3`) appears on the same game.
- The fixed seed and blood icons were re-checked in game 2026-10-30: correct.
- The Queen: assumes she is in the same game location as her door (checked with the game's own location lookup). The log says how many bosses were corrupted when the door opened.
- The corrupted bosses' summons (maddened adds) and extra particles come later.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `OfferingBowl.Start` | Postfix | Registers two RPCs on the altar's view: `corruption_SummonCorrupted` (to the altar's owner) and `corruption_TakeCorruptedOffering` (back to the player who offered) |
| `OfferingBowl.UseItem` | Prefix | A corrupted offering at its altar asks the altar's owner to summon the boss with the one item; normal and wrong items go to the game untouched |
| `OfferingBowl.Interact` | Prefix | Stand altars: all stands corrupted = asks the owner for a corrupted summon; a mix is refused |
| `OfferingBowl.DelayedSpawnBoss` | Postfix | Corrupts the boss just summoned |
| `ItemStand.IsSupported` | Postfix | Stands that take an offering take its corrupted version |
| `ItemStand.Interact` | Prefix | Use on an empty offering stand places a carried corrupted item first |
| `Door.Interact`, `Door.UseItem` | Prefix | A corrupted key opens the door and marks it |
| `Character.OnDeath` | Prefix | Remembers that a corrupted boss died, for its next phase |
| `Character.Awake` | Postfix | Corrupts a corrupted boss's next phase, and bosses behind a corrupted door |

New items `Corrupted<Item>` (cloned from the vanilla item, tinted red). Door data key `corruption_corrupted_door`. The owner's summon calls the game's own `OfferingBowl.CanSpawnBoss`, `SpawnBoss`, `RemoveAltarItems` and its `RPC_BossSpawnInitiated`, in the order `RPC_SpawnBoss` uses (read in 1.0.14; the 1.0.21 body couldn't be read in the cloud session). The `OfferingBowl.RPC_RemoveBossSpawnInventoryItems` prefix is gone (2026-11-08).

## Corrupted bosses (core)

*Corruption plugin, design phase 6, the parts the design fully specifies. Corrupted offerings at the altars (and the corrupted Sealbreaker for the Queen) follow once `altars.csv` from the next `co_dump` shows each altar's real offering; the summons follow phase 4 step B's maddened creatures.*

**What it does in-game:** a corrupted boss is the vanilla boss, tinted red, that:
- deals 1.5× damage and takes 0.7× damage (health unchanged), as the design's starting values;
- **summons** (2026-10-30): every 30 s while players are within 96 m (the army raid's range), 2–3 creatures of the boss's own **army raid** (the same as its biome's rip waves) arrive like a raid, 40–80 m from a player. They're maddened and have **1–2 stars** (user: at least 1; above 2 is for elite packs, later). At most **4 alive per player** near the boss (user). Kall's phases share their summons. Summons are the boss's allies: they go for **players only**, never their boss, each other or other creatures, and can't damage them (user, 2026-10-31). The log names the army and each summon;
- drops 2–3 of its essence instead of 1, and 3–5 corruption shards on every kill, plus its normal vanilla drops.

The flag is stored on the boss itself, so every player sees the same corrupted boss.

**How to test:** `devcommands`, `spawn Eikthyr`, then `co_corrupt` (corrupts the nearest boss within 50 m): it turns red, hits harder, takes longer to kill and drops 2–3 essences and 3–5 shards.

**Config settings** (admin-only unless noted; defaults from `corruption-design.md` → Corrupted bosses):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `DamageDealt` | `1.5` | Damage multiplier on the boss's attacks |
| `DamageTaken` | `0.7` | Damage multiplier on hits the boss takes |
| `EssencesMin` / `EssencesMax` | `2` / `3` | Essences per corrupted kill (replaces the normal amount) |
| `ShardsMin` / `ShardsMax` | `3` / `5` | Shards per corrupted kill, every kill |
| `SummonInterval` | `30` | Seconds between summons (design). 0 = no summons |
| `SummonCountMin` / `SummonCountMax` | `2` / `3` | Creatures per summon (design) |
| `SummonsPerPlayer` | `4` | Most summons alive per player near the boss (user) |
| `SummonMinStars` / `SummonMaxStars` | `1` / `2` | Summons' stars (user: at least 1; above 2 is for elite packs later) |
| `CorruptedBossTintStrength` (Look) | `0.6` | Per player. The Riftforge's tint strength, which was tested in-game |

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-28 (single player): passed.** `co_corrupt` turns Eikthyr red; it hits harder, takes longer to kill and drops 2–3 essences and 3–5 shards. Since 2026-10-30, corrupted offerings summon them too (see [Corrupted offerings](#corrupted-offerings)).
- **Multiplayer fix 2026-11-01 (untested):** the limit now counts summons this game spawned even when they're far from it, so it holds with players spread out.
- **Play-test 2026-10-31 (single player, Eikthyr and Bonemass): passed.** Summons arrive every 30 s from the army, 2–3 at a time, up to 4 alive alone, and stop when the boss dies. **Fixed after the test:** summons attacked Eikthyr when the player was away and he attacked them (maddened creatures treat everything as an enemy); now summons and their boss are allies and summons target players only. **Re-checked 2026-10-31: passed** (phone play-test log). The Ashlands army's charred spawner stones aren't summoned (only creatures count towards the limit).
- The design's extra particles (Bonemass poison cloud, Ashlands embers) aren't added yet.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Character.Awake` | Postfix | Adds a watcher to bosses that tints them red once they're corrupted |
| `Character.RPC_Damage` | Prefix | Scales hits on and from corrupted bosses |
| `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList` | Postfix (the existing shard and essence ones) | Corrupted bosses: 3–5 shards and 2–3 essences |

## Inventory tabs

*Corruption plugin, design phase 9. Behaviour decided by the user, 2026-10-29 (Currency and Maps); six tabs by the user's round brief of 2026-11-09 (issue #30).*

**What it does:** extra tabs on the player inventory, so currency, splinters, maps and essences don't take bag space.

- **Tab buttons** **Bag · Currencies · Splinters · Maps · Runes · Essences** (that order, the user's) sit on top of the inventory panel, sharing its width (narrower than the old three; long names shrink to fit). A tab shows in place of the bag (the character sheet and equipment slots stay put); the inventory opens on Bag each time.
- **Currencies** (8 × 4): the Corruption shard and the ten currency orbs, **each in a fixed cell**: shard, Transmutation, Augmentation, Alchemy, Regal, Exalted, Divine, Annulment (top row), Chance, Jeweller's, Mirror. When you have none of one, its cell stays, showing the material dimmed with a **0** and a "none left" tooltip. No capacity is lost: a second stack of the same orb, or anything else that ends up here, goes to the free cells no material owns, and only when those are full into the cells of materials you have none of. Only positions change, never items or counts. Dragging an orb elsewhere in the tab is allowed, but it goes back to its cell once dropped.
- **Splinters** (8 × 4, the size of the game's own bag, without EquipmentAndQuickSlots' or a backpack's rows): a plain bag-style grid for Affix Splinters, one per cell, never stacked. No scrolling, search or fixed places: the limit is on purpose, chests hold collections (dragging to and from a chest keeps every item's saved data). **The splinter item doesn't exist yet** (#29); it must be named `CorruptionAffixSplinter…` (`ShatterRules.SplinterPrefab`), and it goes here then.
- **Maps** (8 × 4): rip maps, as before.
- **Runes:** only a note, "Runes are not available yet." There's no grid and no inventory behind it, so nothing can be put there by any route (dragging, pickup, the half-second tidy, chests, overflow, migration), and no item name sends anything there. Its save key (`corruption_tab_runes`) is reserved and never read or written; if a save has something there it's left untouched (the note says so).
- **Essences** (8 × 4): the boss essences, each in a fixed cell in `essences.csv` order, stacking, dimmed with a 0 when you have none. **Moved out of Currencies:** essences an older version saved in Currencies move to Essences when the character loads (logged), and again every half second if Essences was full; only between tabs that loaded completely, and only when the item fits, using the game's own move, so nothing is lost or doubled however often it runs.
- **Straight to the tab:** picking up (by hand or auto-pickup) puts each item in its tab, even when the bag is full. Only a full tab sends it to the bag. Anything that still lands in the bag (crafted at the Riftforge, dragged in, taken from a chest, from an older save) moves to its tab within half a second, and so does anything sitting in the wrong tab. EquipmentAndQuickSlots' quick and equipment slots are left alone. Items are recognised by their prefab name (`CorruptionShard`, `CorruptionOrb_*`, `CorruptionEssence_*`, `CorruptionAffixSplinter*`, `CorruptionMap_*`), so new rows in the data tables go to their tab without code changes.
- **Weightless:** tab contents don't count towards carry weight.
- **Still usable:** the tabs count and pay as part of your inventory for every recipe (Riftforge maps, building costs, boss altars), and pay before the bag. Right-click a map in the Maps tab to open its rip. Drag between a tab and a chest, shift-click to send an item to an open chest, split stacks, hover for tooltips, as in the bag. A tab only takes its own items.
- **Death:** every tab's contents go into the gravestone with the rest of your inventory, in rows under the bag's (world settings that keep or delete items on death apply to them too). Taking everything from the gravestone puts them back in their tabs; they don't count against the gravestone's "room and weight" check.
- Saved with the character (in its custom data, one key per tab: `corruption_tab_currency` and `corruption_tab_maps` as before, new `corruption_tab_splinters` and `corruption_tab_essences`), so they come with you between worlds and servers like the bag. Only you see your tabs.

**Config settings** (admin-only, synced from the server):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Enabled` | `true` | Off: the tab buttons are hidden and the tabs' contents are kept (not lost) but can't be reached or used until it's turned on again |

Tab size (8 × 4) is fixed by the user's choice; which item goes where (never Runes), the fixed cells and the gravestone layout are unit-tested (`TabRulesTests` against the real `maps.csv` and `essences.csv`, `FixedLayoutTests`).

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-30 (single player):** the tab buttons and switching work (step 1 passed), and the bag's maps moved to the Maps tab. But the tab grids were broken: every cell after the first showed a placeholder (a "1/2" icon on yellow, a "Tooltip" text), nothing could be dragged, dropped or moved out of a tab, and a crafted map seemed to vanish (it was in the Maps tab, just not drawn). Cause: the copied grid lacked six of the eight callbacks the game gives its own grids; the missing drop check (`CanDropDragOntoItem`) threw a NullReferenceException right after the first item on every draw. **Fixed the same day** (all callbacks wired; awaiting re-test). Also changed: tabs fill from the top-left (the game puts materials in the bottom row first).
- **Re-test 2026-10-30 after the fix:** the grids draw correctly and fill from the top-left; crafting a map from Currency-tab shards and an essence puts the map in the Maps tab; right-clicking a map there opens its rip; dragging to and from a chest, shift-click to a chest and refusing wood work; no errors. Pickup into the tabs with a full bag and backpack works. **Still failing then:** left-click couldn't pick up an item in a tab unless a container (the backpack) was open (Ctrl+click to drop worked). Cause: with no container open, the game cancels every frame any drag whose item isn't in the bag (`InventoryGui.UpdateContainer`, also `CloseContainer`). The first fix guarded `UpdateInventory` by mistake and changed nothing (second re-test, same day: still failing); now `UpdateContainer`. **Third re-test 2026-10-30: passed** (pick up, move within a tab, drop on the ground with nothing open). Every Session 1e step has passed in single player. **Second re-test 2026-10-30: death and gravestone, relogging, and picking up with a full bag pass.**
- Right-clicking a map in the Maps tab reached the rip code; the rip didn't open only because the test world had no unexplored Meadows left (the map then stays, as designed). Re-test in a world with unexplored land (or after `resetmap`).
- Mouse only: the tab grid isn't part of the game's gamepad navigation.
- Auto-pickup still checks the item's weight against your carry limit, so when you're overloaded it skips currency (picking up with E works).
- With a tab full, extra items stay in the bag; when space frees up they move over on their own.
- A dropped item can't swap a non-tab item into a tab (the drop is refused).
- **To watch the essence move:** `co_tabs_misplace [count]` (needs `devcommands`) puts Eikthyr essences straight into Currencies, as older saves hold them; they should move to Essences within half a second.
- **Play-test 2026-11-10 (single player): passed** (checklist A–C, E: six tabs in order, nothing lost, essences moved to Essences, fixed currency places and counts, Splinters refusing other items, Runes taking nothing, death and gravestone, relog). Real splinters wait for #29.
- **Six tabs (2026-11-09):** The fixed cells are re-arranged on the frame after a change, never inside the game's own inventory code (a drag-and-drop swap is several steps; moving items between them could lose one), and not while something is being dragged. Going back to an older version of the mod hides the Splinters and Essences tabs' contents (they're kept in the save, under keys the older version doesn't read).
- **A tab that can't be loaded is locked, not lost (2026-11-07, untested):** each tab loads on its own into a scratch inventory and is shown only if every saved item arrived. If a tab's saved data is damaged, or an item in it no longer exists (e.g. a mod that adds it isn't loaded), that tab is locked for the session: a message says so on arrival (details in the BepInEx log), its button shows the message instead of the tab, nothing is spent from it or put in it (pickups go to the bag), it isn't put in the gravestone, and its saved data is kept exactly as it was, so it comes back once the cause is fixed. The other tab works normally. Before this, one damaged tab stopped *both* from saving while still showing them: currency spent from the good tab came back after a reload, and new pickups into either tab were lost. Tested with a stand-in of the character file (`TabStoreTests`). **Play-test 2026-11-07 (single player): passed** (locked Maps and Currency, spending, pickups to the bag, undo all as expected); the message ran off the screen (one long line; the game's centre message doesn't wrap), now three short lines: re-check passed the same day ("showing good"). **To try it:** `co_tabs_damage <currency|splinters|maps|essences> <missing|garbled|undo>` (needs `devcommands`) replaces that tab's saved data with a damaged copy (`missing`: the tab's items plus a shard pointing at a prefab that doesn't exist; `garbled`: cut-off data), keeping the original under `corruption_tab_<tab>_backup`, and locks the tab at once so this session's saves don't overwrite it; log out and back in to see the result. `undo` puts the original back (log out and back in again).

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):** all act only on the local player's own inventory.

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `Player.Load` | Prefix + Postfix | Prefix: locks every tab (so a failed load can't be saved over). Postfix: reads each tab from the character's custom data (`corruption_tab_currency`, `corruption_tab_splinters`, `corruption_tab_maps`, `corruption_tab_essences`; never `corruption_tab_runes`) into a scratch inventory and unlocks it only if every item arrived; then moves essences from Currencies to Essences |
| `Player.Save` | Prefix | Writes the unlocked tabs into the character's custom data; a locked tab's saved data is left as it was |
| `Inventory.AddItem(ItemData)` | Prefix, high priority (skips the original) | Puts currency and maps in their tab when it has room (pickup, take from a chest) |
| `Inventory.CanAddItem(ItemData, int)` | Postfix, low priority | A full bag can still take currency and maps while their tab has room |
| `Inventory.MoveAll` | Prefix | "Take all" (gravestone, chest): currency and maps to their tabs first |
| `Player.Update` | Postfix | Local player: after a tab changed, puts Currencies' and Essences' materials in their fixed cells (not while dragging); twice a second, moves tab items from the bag's rows to their tab and items in the wrong tab to theirs |
| `Inventory.CountItems(string, int, bool)` | Postfix | Adds the tabs' stock |
| `Inventory.HaveItem(string, bool)` | Postfix | Also true when only a tab has the item |
| `Inventory.RemoveItem(string, int, int, bool)` | Prefix, high priority (changes `amount`) | Pays from the tabs first; does nothing if an earlier prefix already skipped the original |
| `Player.CreateTombStone` | Prefix | Moves the tabs' items into rows under the bag, so the gravestone takes them like everything else |
| `Inventory.MoveInventoryToGrave` | Postfix | Gives the gravestone enough rows for them |
| `TombStone.EasyFitInInventory` | Postfix | Leaves tab items (which go to the tabs and weigh nothing) out of the room and weight check |
| `InventoryGui.Show` / `Hide` / `UpdateInventory` / `OnDestroy` | Postfix | Creates the six tab buttons, the grid and the Runes note, resets to Bag on close (not when the Riftforge workspace kept the window open), keeps the shown tab's grid current and draws the dimmed cells of materials you have none of |
| `InventoryGrid.DropItem` | Prefix | Refuses items that don't belong in the tab, and swaps that would put a non-tab item in a tab |
| `Inventory.TopFirst` | Postfix | Tabs fill from the top-left |
| `InventoryGui.UpdateContainer`, `InventoryGui.CloseContainer` | Prefix + Finalizer | Mark the two places where the game drops drags from non-bag inventories when no container is open |
| `InventoryGui.SetupDragItem` | Prefix (skips the original) | Within those two, keeps a drag that came from a tab |
| `InventoryGui.OnSelectedItem` | Prefix (our grid only) | Shift-click in a tab with a chest open moves the item to the chest; does nothing when an earlier prefix (the Riftforge workspace) already handled the click |

Also changed: using a map (`Humanoid.UseItem` prefix in `Rips.cs`) accepts maps in the Maps tab, and opening the rip takes the map from the bag or the tab. *Since 2026-11-27 it uses up the exact map used (or an identical one) in the inventory it was used from.*

**Conflicts:** see `docs/mod-compatibility.md`. EquipmentAndQuickSlots also patches `AddItem`, `CanAddItem`, `MoveInventoryToGrave`, `EasyFitInInventory` and the gravestone's height; AdventureBackpacks and CraftingOverhaul's craft-from-chests also patch `CountItems` and `RemoveItem`. Payment order: tabs, then AdventureBackpacks' backpack, then nearby chests, then the bag.
