## Riftforge workspace

*Corruption plugin, ROADMAP U3, issue #30. Design: the user's round brief of 2026-11-09 (replacing the two-slot, operation-list, preview-and-confirm window); contract in `docs/riftforge-ui-brief.md`; mock-ups `docs/images/riftforge_workspace.png` and `riftforge_workspace_shatter.png` (`tools/mockup_riftforge_workspace.py`).*

**What it does:** crafting with orbs at the Riftforge, straight from your inventory. Crafting only works here (no crafting anywhere else).

- **A third tab, "Forge"**, next to the Riftforge's Craft and Upgrade tabs (a copy of the game's tab button, in the same capitals). It only appears while you use a Riftforge. Craft and Upgrade still work; pressing them leaves the Forge tab.
- **Looks like the station's own tabs** (redone 2026-11-09 after Krogon's first look: the first version "looks very out of place"). While Forge is open, the station's recipe list and item description are hidden (not changed; they come back when you leave the tab) and replaced in the same places by:
  - **the bench**, where the recipe list was, in the list's own dark frame: a "Forge" heading, the **forge slot** (one of the bag's own cells, half as big again), the gear's name, then **its affix block** (the rarity line, every affix with its value, bonus and range, and the Corrupted/Mirrored marks) in large, well-spaced text in a fixed place, each affix behind a bullet with its wrapped lines indented (a resonant affix is green with a green ◆; the Corrupted and Mirrored marks centred under them; added 2026-11-10: "it looks like a wall of text"), since players read it before every click; at the bottom a **Remove** button (a copy of the game's Craft button). Changed 2026-11-10 at Krogon's request: the affixes were small text at the bottom of the description. **Changed 2026-11-28 (Krogon, decision 25 on `claude/wizardly-wozniak-4aix76`, his mockup; built, not play-tested): for gear the bench shows the name, the rarity line, the marks and the item's own description; the affix list moved to the sheet.**
  - **a copy of the game's description panel**, where the description was, without its icon (it repeated the forge slot): the gear's name and its **routine stats** (the tooltip without the affix block) at the game's own size, as in the Craft tab. Where the game's cost slots were (they meant nothing here and are gone), the **result of the last use** runs across the panel's width; the panel's main button is **Shatter**. With nothing in the forge it explains the steps. **Changed 2026-11-28 (decision 25): for gear the sheet shows the name and the affix list (bulleted, the last use's new lines highlighted), like a map's summary; the routine stats are gone** (Krogon: "vanilla stats can be easily seen when hovering over the item anyway").
  - The game's description font everywhere; no new art.
- **The forge slot** points at the gear: drag gear from your Bag onto it (pick the item up, click the slot); the item stays in your bag, so closing the window never needs anything recovered. Right-click the slot or press **Remove** to empty it. It empties by itself when the item is destroyed or leaves your bag (dropped, moved to a chest). Only gear that can carry affixes goes in ("This item can't be worked at the Riftforge"); items from a tab or a chest are refused ("Put in gear from your Bag"). A gold frame shows round the slot while the picked orb can be used on the gear.
- **Pick and click:** right-click an orb in Currencies (or one waiting in the bag because Currencies was full) to pick it: it follows the cursor with your count, and its cell is highlighted; the forge slot gets a gold frame while the orb can be used on the gear. Left-click the slot to use it **once**. Hold **Shift** while clicking to keep the pick for the next click (it moves on to your next stack of that orb). Right-clicking another material replaces the pick. **Escape**, a right-click, or a left-click on any inventory cell drops the pick and does nothing else (no pick-up, move, equip or use). A chest's orbs are never picked.
- **What can be used shows in the tabs:** while gear is in the slot, materials that can't be used on it are tinted red; materials you have none of are dimmed with a 0 (Inventory tabs). The pick line and the result line say why a material can't be used ("Only on Normal items", the Jeweller's Orb's "Sockets come later", …).
- **After each use:** a short result under the stats, a headline then the changed lines, e.g. "Regal Orb: now Rare, 1 affix added" / "+ +12% attack speed" in green (a line gone shows in red with −); the new or rerolled line is also **highlighted in the affix block** for 6 seconds. Nothing waits on it: the next click works at once. The stats, counts and red tints update; the gear stays in the slot. A refusal shows in red in the same place. A refusal says why and uses nothing. Every rule, and the "Use this at a Riftforge" check, is `RiftforgeCrafting`'s, checked again on every click. **Changed 2026-11-28 (decisions 24 and 26, built, not play-tested): no result text in the panel at all**, for gear and maps; the highlight shows what was added, a removed line simply goes. Centre-screen toasts: "Your item was destroyed" (red, a shard), "Your item was shattered" (orange), "Your item was corrupted" (red, a shard that added no affix), "Your item was mirrored" (orange). A refusal found out by clicking (no room for the copy, not in the bag, none left, the splinters couldn't be stored, no confirm box) shows as the game's small top-left message; picking an orb that can't be used shows nothing (it is already tinted red).
- **Shatter button, shards and splinters (built 2026-11-10, #29):** see [Riftforge crafting: shards, shattering and Affix Splinters](#riftforge-crafting-shards-shattering-and-affix-splinters). Shatter and a shard each ask in the Forge's own dialog (Cancel / Shatter, Cancel / Corrupt; Escape closes it without doing anything), each with a "Don't warn me again" tickbox; splinters apply at once, like orbs. The Shatter button is grey while the rules refuse the gear; when only room is missing it stays live and says "Make room for up to X splinters."
- **Outside the Forge tab** every click behaves exactly as before; nothing can be crafted.
- **Maps (built 2026-11-27, Forge tab tested alone on Valheim 1.0.31 the same day):** the forge slot also takes a map, from the bag or the Maps tab (drag it, or right-click it while the Forge tab is open); the orbs and the shard then work on it as on gear. The shard on a full tier 5 map asks nothing: one click makes it tier 6 (decision 14). Since 2026-11-27 (decision 15, tested alone the same day) a map in the forge shows its tier line under the slot and its summary on the right, the result moves to a band at the bottom of the right column, and **Shatter is hidden**; gear keeps the layout above. See [Map tiers and affixes](#map-tiers-and-affixes).

No config settings. The click rules are unit-tested (`ForgeRulesTests`).

**Known issues:**
- **Play-test 2026-11-10 (single player): passed** (checklist D: putting gear in, picking and using orbs, Shift, Escape, refusals, Jeweller's, Mirror, Remove, closing, away from the Riftforge). Then look tweaks asked and made, and confirmed the same day ("it looks good"): the last empty box in the right panel (the Upgrade tab's item frame, copied with the panel) is hidden along with anything else left in that row; the forge slot gets a frame from the station's tab art; half a line of space between the rarity line and the affixes.
- **Second look 2026-11-10 (Krogon, screenshots):** "much better, a lot more consistent", it feels good, Craft and Upgrade come back, all six tabs show, essences moved. Asked: readable affixes in a fixed place, a full-width short result with the changed affix highlighted, no unexplained empty boxes, no repeated icon, no "Right-click an orb" line → done the same day (above); untested.
- **First look 2026-11-09 (Krogon, screenshot):** a few orbs worked ("they do work"); the window looked out of place (its own dark box over the station's list, with the list showing through, and the tooltip in the game's capital-letter heading font) → redone the same day from the game's own panel parts, as above; untested. "Take out" renamed "Remove".
- Built against the game's code (the decompiled 1.0 assemblies), checked by compiling. If another mod rebuilds the crafting panel so its list and description can't be told apart, the Forge tab isn't added and the log says why.
- Mouse only (no gamepad).
- The Mirror of Kalandra's copy goes in the bag; the original stays in the slot.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):** all act only on the local player's own inventory and only while the Forge tab is open, unless stated.

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `InventoryGui.UpdateCraftingPanel` | Postfix | Creates the Forge tab and its view once (from copies of the game's tab button, description panel and Craft button); keeps the Forge tab selected while it's open |
| `ItemData.GetTooltip` (the loot tooltip's existing postfix) | — | The same affix section in every tooltip and on the bench (`LootTooltip.Section`); the Forge tab asks it once in a while to leave the section out of the description (the bench shows it) |
| `InventoryGui.OnTabCraftPressed`, `InventoryGui.OnTabUpgradePressed` | Prefix | Leave the Forge tab (always, reads only) |
| `InventoryGui.Update` | Prefix + Postfix | Prefix: Escape drops the pick (or closes the Yes/No box) instead of closing the inventory. Postfix (always): shows the Forge tab only at a Riftforge; keeps the slot, pick, cursor and tooltip current |
| `InventoryGui.Hide` | Prefix (can skip the original) + Postfix | Prefix: skips closing only in the frame Escape dropped a pick. Postfix: leaves the Forge tab and empties the slot (a reference only) |
| `InventoryGui.OnRightClickItem` | Prefix, first (can skip the original) | Right-click on your own orb, shard or splinter picks it instead of using it; with a pick, any other right-click only drops it. *Since 2026-11-27 (built, untested) a right-clicked own map, with the Forge tab open, goes into the forge instead of opening a rip* |
| `InventoryGui.OnSelectedItem` | Prefix, first (can skip the original) | With a pick and nothing dragged, a left-click on any inventory cell only drops the pick |
| `InventoryGui.OnDestroy` | Postfix | Forgets the workspace's objects |

Also: after the game draws the grids each frame (`InventoryGui.UpdateInventory` postfix of the Inventory tabs), the workspace highlights the picked cell and tints the materials that can't be used.

Also: while Forge is open, the station's list and description get a `CanvasGroup` (invisible, no clicks), removed or restored when you leave; CraftingOverhaul's search box and grouping button (objects named `CraftingOverhaul_*`) are hidden the same way. No game method is changed for this.

**Conflicts:** see `docs/mod-compatibility.md`. CraftingOverhaul's recipe search and categories also patch the crafting panel (`UpdateRecipeList`, `Show`, `Hide`); the Forge view hides the list while open and leaves it otherwise alone.

## Odin items unlocked

*Corruption plugin. User, 2026-10-31.*

**What it does:** the game gates the Odin cape, the Odin helmet and the Odin tankard behind a Steam entitlement (`SharedData.m_dlc` = `"beta"`, the only such tag in 1.0.20). Without it the game refuses to equip them, hides their recipes and won't craft them, and their tooltip says "DLC item". Corruption clears the tag on every item at startup, so they work like any other item for everyone: equip, craft at the workbench, and roll as loot (they're in `loot_bases.csv`). The log lists each item it unlocked.

**Why:** a play-test rolled a Rare Odin cape ("DLC required"); the user chose to unlock them fully rather than leave them out of the loot.

**Known issues:** tested 2026-10-31: the items equip, the log lists all three as unlocked and the game announces their recipes as new; crafting one not tried yet. Needs Corruption on every machine (already required).

**Vanilla behaviour overridden:** no Harmony patch; the items' shared data is changed once at startup (`PrefabManager.OnVanillaPrefabsAvailable`).

---

## Loot system

*Corruption plugin, design phase 7, step 1 of several: drops, tooltips and item-stat affixes. Orbs, uniques, personal-loot locks, loot beams, player-stat, gathering and proc effects come in later steps.*

**What it does in-game:**
- **Magic and Rare drops.** Every creature kill (not tamed creatures or players) rolls for loot, once **per player within the game's nearby-players range** (`Game.m_difficultyScaleRange`, the range the game counts players in to scale creature difficulty; 100 m in the 1.0 code). Chances come from `drop_chances.csv` (Magic 1 in 50, Rare 1 in 100) times `drop_modifiers.csv`: stars (`star_1`–`star_3`), rip creatures (`rip_tier_1`; maps have no tiers yet), corrupted bosses (uniques only). If Magic and Rare both succeed, only the Rare drops (`DropTable.Roll`). Unique rolls are ignored until uniques exist.
- **Which item:** a random player-craftable weapon, shield, armour piece, cape (backpacks included), trinket or pickaxe **from the biome the creature died in**; with **20%** chance one from the biome just before it (user, 2026-10-29). Meadows always gives Meadows items; the Ocean gives none. The list is `loot_bases.csv`, built from the game dump by `tools/make_loot_bases.py` (249 items since the 1.0.31 dump of 2026-11-27, 248 before; the biome is the item's own from the dump, e.g. gem-socketed Ashlands weapons count as Deep North because their recipe needs a Deep North material).
- **Upgrade level 1**, full durability, like a freshly crafted item (user). Upgrading at the workbench or forge keeps the affixes.
- **Affix count:** Magic 1 or 2 (1 twice as often), Rare 3 or 4 (3 twice as often) (user: fewer is likelier). Magic holds at most 1 prefix + 1 suffix, Rare 2 + 2.
- **Rolling** (`AffixRoller`): each affix is picked by its `weight` among every affix that fits the item's classes and mechanics (`requires`), one per family per side; values are whole numbers anywhere in the range (every range in the table is whole). Both halves of a twin merge into one **resonant** line rolled from the resonant range (+25% on the combined top). **Every affix in `affixes.csv` rolls, including rows still marked `draft`** (user, 2026-10-29: test with the draft numbers while the ranges are settled).
- **Names:** Magic items take their affixes' names around the base name, the roll deciding which of a family's names (e.g. *Sharp/Fine/Cruel/Merciless*): "Cruel Bronze Sword of Embers". Rare items get a two-word name from `rare_names.csv` ("Grim Fang"). Names are blue (Magic, `#8888FF`) or yellow (Rare, `#FFFF77`), the Path of Exile colours, in inventory tooltips and when hovering over the item on the ground.
- **Tooltips:** after vanilla's lines, the rarity (and for Rares the base item), then one line per affix: its value, the bonus it gives this item at its current level (item-stat affixes, e.g. `(+14)`), and its roll range in brackets, e.g. `+24% damage (+14) [5–40]`; resonant lines end in `◆ resonant`. Affixes this step doesn't apply yet are grey and say "(not active yet)". **Layout since 2026-11-10** (Krogon, the same as the Riftforge's Forge bench): a little space under the rarity line, each affix behind a bullet with its wrapped lines indented, a resonant affix green with a green ◆ instead of the word, the Corrupted/Mirrored marks centred.
- **Player-stat affixes (step 2, 2026-10-30; tested 2026-10-31 except weapon skill and life steal; life steal passed on a bow 2026-11-07 and with a melee weapon 2026-11-15; weapon skill's check for bug #56 passed 2026-11-26):** summed over everything the local player has equipped (weapons in hand, armour, cape or backpack, utility, trinket; sheathed weapons don't count): max health, stamina and eitr (added to the food values), health and stamina regen (+X% adds X/100 to the regen multiplier, as vanilla's bonuses do), movement speed worn and held (Fleet) under the 35% cap (added to the gear movement modifier, like the Crown of Valheim), carry weight (scaled by the world's carry setting), fire/frost/lightning/poison resistance plus all resistances (each element capped at 75%, applied on top of vanilla resistances to hits the player takes), weapon skill levels for the weapon in hand, life steal (capped at 10%, from the hit's damage before the target's armour). No caps on carry weight, regen or max health/stamina/eitr (user). **Known issue, weapon skill ([#56](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/56)):** play-test 2026-10-31, an axe with +7 to axes showed +6 in the Skills tab. **Confirmed a display quirk only** (Krogon's check, phone page, 2026-11-26, step `stat-skill-56` passed: `co_affix weapon_skill_prefix 7`, then `raiseskill Axes 0.6` showed +6 and `raiseskill Axes 0.4` showed +7): after a death skill levels are fractional, and the game's Skills tab shows the bonus as the rounded-down total minus the fractional level, so the full +7 applies but shows as +6 until the level is whole again. A vanilla skill bonus shows the same. Whether to correct the number on screen is Krogon's call (#56).
- **Gathering-tool affixes, striker side (step 3, 2026-11-01; tested in single player 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-11, and Keen-eyed, Magnetic, Swift and Scholar's on 2026-11-26 (phone page `g3c-11`, `g3c-12`); Splintering's reach raised to 4 m on 2026-11-19, untested):** changes to the local player's own swing before the hit goes to the object's owner, so they work in multiplayer. Prospector's (+% pickaxe damage to rock and ore), Veinbreaker's (every 5th mining swing with the tool; the count starts over whenever the tool is unequipped, for any reason: switching items, the hide-weapons key, swimming, eating with it in hand, dropping, storing or breaking it; Krogon 2026-11-08), Seismic (a chance the swing also hits every deposit part touching the one struck; the touching parts are read before the hit is sent, so it works the same whoever's game runs the deposit), **Odin's Grace on rock and ore** (a chance the struck part and every part touching it break at once; one-piece rocks break outright; the tool tier still applies), Excavator's (a deeper and wider dig: the pickaxe's own terrain change scaled), Lumberjack's (standing trees), Timberfall (fallen logs), Rootbreaker (stumps: the prefabs the game's trees leave, `TreeBase.m_stubPrefab`), Splintering (a chance the same hit also lands on the nearest other tree or log within 4 m of the spot hit, `loot_rules.csv` → `splintering_reach_m`; Krogon 2026-11-19, before the axe's own reach of about 2 m), Fit (a chance a swing costs no stamina), Careful (a chance a hit costs no durability), Hushed (the noise of your own swings and hits), Light (lighter tool), Keen-eyed and Magnetic (pickup radius while the tool is held, capped at 8 m), Swift (faster swing animation, capped with the other attack-speed affixes; other players see it too), Scholar's (more Woodcutting and Pickaxes skill XP). Seismic, Odin's Grace and Splintering roll once per swing. Durable and Efficient already worked (durability, attack stamina). The on-harvest effects and Odin's Grace on trees: next bullet.
- **Gathering-tool affixes, on harvest (step 3, second half, 2026-11-01, untested):** a *harvest* is every object or deposit part that drops resources when it breaks (a tree, each log, a stump, a rock, each deposit chunk, including chunks that fall when the hit leaves them unsupported); every chance rolls once per harvest. Both Bountifuls (universal: the harvest's drops all drop once more; woodcutting: its wood once more; both can trigger, triple wood), Determined (each harvest restores X% of max stamina, on the striker), Artisan (1 more of the ore that dropped), Gemseeker's (1 gem: Amber or Amber pearl from Meadows deposits, also Ruby from the Black Forest on, in biome-detection order, so the Ocean counts as after the Swamp; equally likely), **Hoardfinder's and of Hidden Riches find orbs (Krogon, 2026-11-19; renamed from Stonecutter's and Resinous the same day):** a mining harvest (a rock or deposit part your hit breaks) with Hoardfinder's, or a woodcutting harvest (a tree, log or stump) with of Hidden Riches, has the affix's chance (1–5%) to drop one orb, always, picked with the orb table's odds as weights, so mostly Transmutation and Augmentation, now and then a rare one, never the Jeweller's ([Currency orb drops](#currency-orb-drops)); it drops with the harvest, spawned by the object's owner. Tools rolled before 2026-11-19 keep their old 5–25% value until a Divine Orb rerolls it. **Ids (2026-11-19, decision 13):** `hoardfinders` and `hidden_riches`, effects and families `orb_find_mining` and `orb_find_woodcutting` (were `stonecutters` / `resinous`, `extra_stone` / `extra_resin`). Saved gear and splinters with the old ids read as the new ones (`Corruption/Rules/AffixRenames.cs`); the old splinter items `CorruptionAffixSplinter_tool_extra_stone` / `_extra_resin` stay registered with the new look so saved splinters still load, and new splinters use `…_tool_orb_find_mining` / `…_orb_find_woodcutting`. `co_affix` takes the old ids too. Replaces one stone or resin per hit (2026-11-08); the affix ids stay, so saved gear and splinters keep working. Forester's (1 of the seed in the tree's own drop table; trees without one don't trigger it), Charring (each wood item that drops, doubled ones too, rolls on its own and drops as coal instead). **Odin's Grace on trees:** a chance the strike harvests the whole tree at once: hitting a standing tree drops its own drops, its log, everything the log would split into and its stump; hitting a log or stump harvests every loaded log and stump of the same tree plus what each log would still split into. Logs and stumps of trees felled before this version have no tree ID: there it harvests the piece hit and its splits. Every piece is a harvest (all the effects above roll for each) and must pass the tool tier. Rolls once per swing, for the first tree, log or stump hit. **What counts:** wood is what the game's logs and stumps drop, seeds are what planting a sapling or crop costs, ores are what the coal-fired smelters take (all read from the game's data at startup and listed in the log). Mining harvests are rocks and deposits broken by pickaxe damage, woodcutting harvests trees, logs and stumps broken by chop damage. **How:** the striker sends the object's owner its tool's chances before each hit; the owner applies them to the drop list and spawns everything itself (DECISIONS → Architecture, 2026-11-01). **Only your swings (Krogon, 2026-11-08):** the chances apply only to a hit a player made (the swing, or a copy of it: Seismic, Splintering, Odin's Grace, the hit a breaking rock passes to its copper deposit). Something that breaks without a player's hit (a log rolling or landing, a falling tree knocking another apart, a creature's hit) gives normal drops, even if you hit it earlier; the owner's log says `Harvest affixes: <object> was hit without a player's swing`. Before, such breaks kept your affixes only when your own game ran the object. **Play-test 2026-11-08 (single player):** these fixes, Seismic and Odin's Grace on your own deposits and Veinbreaker's reset passed; with another player's game running the object not tried yet (Session 2, 2f). **Known issues:** a pickaxe breaking a one-piece object with drops (e.g. a barrel) counts as a mining harvest, as Prospector's already treats it; the whole-tree harvest skips the vanilla tree statistics; **to check with two players (Session 2, 2f):** two players with Odin's Grace hitting pieces of the same felled tree at the same moment, each piece run by a different player's game, may both harvest the whole tree (drops twice). Each whole-tree harvest now logs `Odin's Grace: whole-tree harvest of tree …` with how many pieces another player's game ran; not reproduced yet, so not fixed.
- **Proc and utility affixes (step 4, 2026-11-01, untested; `LootProcs`, rules in `ProcRules`):** numbers from each row's text and notes in `affixes.csv` (a test checks they still match).
  - **Parry** = vanilla's perfect block: the blocking item (shield, or the weapon without one) has a parry bonus, the block started under 0.25 s before the hit, and the block held (stamina left, guard not broken), the case where vanilla plays its perfect-block effect. The blocking item's affixes count.
  - **Shieldwall** (+X% armour, 10 s), **Rallying** (+X% damage on your next hit, 10 s or until a hit lands; every enemy that swing or shot hits in that frame gets it), **Skald's** (+X% attack speed, 10 s) and **Steadfast** (X stamina at once) reach you and every player within 10 m, through the game's own status-effect message (`SEMan.AddStatusEffect` → `RPC_AddStatusEffect`), the rolled value carried as its skill-level number. Each player's own game applies it. A new parry restarts the time; a weaker one keeps the stronger value. Icons copied from vanilla status effects (Magic barrier, Dvergr power, Berserkir Mead; Steadfast has none).
  - **Reprisal:** X% chance per parry: lightning worth 50% of the blocking item's block power (its own block stat at its upgrade level, block affix included, not the Blocking skill) hits the attacker, with Dundr's bolt-hit effect (`StaffLightning`'s projectile). Credited to you (kills, XP).
  - **Wrath:** on the last hit of the weapon's combo (vanilla's own test, the hit it doubles), once per swing, every enemy within 3 m of the hit, the struck one included, takes X% of that hit's damage (chop and pickaxe left out), pushed away with the weapon's own knockback; Stagbreaker's slam effect.
  - **Skadi:** each hit on the same enemy adds a stack (the first hit has 1, up to 5); each stack adds X% of the hit's physical damage as frost. Another target, another weapon or 3 s without a hit starts again at 1. The frost is part of the hit's single damage number (the game shows no separate frost number), and at these amounts its slowing effect lasts only a blink, so Skadi can't be seen in play: with `devcommands` on, each Skadi hit says "Skadi: stack N, +X frost (weapon)" at the top left (testing aid, 2026-11-07).
  - **Bloodforged** (heal X% of max health) and **Fenrir** (+X% attack speed for 10 s; each kill adds a stack up to 3 and restarts the time; every stack gives the latest kill's value): the creature's owner sends `Corruption_LootKill` to the killer's game when a creature (not a player, not tamed, as for drops) dies from the player's hit. Skald's and Fenrir add to Swift under the 30% attack-speed cap.
  - **Deathless:** damage that would kill you leaves 1 health instead (fall damage and damage over time too), then the cooldown: the shortest rolled one among your Deathless items (several never stack). The start time is kept in the character's custom data (`corruption.deathless_used`, world time), so it survives relogging; a "Deathless spent" icon (vanilla's No skill drain) counts it down. Works before the game's death check, so EquipmentAndQuickSlots' keep-on-death never starts.
  - **Foraging:** picking a wild plant gives +X% movement speed for 8 s (several Foraging pieces add up), under the 35% movement cap with the other movement affixes; another pick restarts it (PlantEasily's bulk harvest counts as one pick). Wild plant = a pickable that grows back (`m_respawnTimeMinutes` > 0) and isn't a crop players grow (`Plant.m_grownPrefabs`); the list goes to the log at startup ("Foraging: N wild plants").
  - Proc hits (Reprisal, Wrath) never backstab and don't trigger Skadi, Rallying or Wrath; knockback and life steal affixes still apply to them, those of the proc's own item (Wrath: the weapon; Reprisal: the blocking item).
  - **Damage over time triggers no on-hit affix (2026-11-18, Krogon: "Blast counts, ticks don't"; tested in-game the same day, single player, with life steal on Venom Strike's cloud and Immolation Arrow's ground):** a hit typed as burning or poison (`HitSources.IsDamageOverTime`) skips knockback, backstab, life steal (`LootHitEffects`) and Skadi, Rallying and the procs (`LootProcs`). The game's own burning and poison never come through `Character.Damage`; Orlog's burning ground and poison cloud (Immolation Arrow, Venom Strike) send their ticks typed so. Immolation Arrow's blast still counts, as a hit of the bow.
  - **Which item a hit's affixes come from (2026-11-07, untested in game):** knockback, backstab, life steal and Skadi use the item the hit comes from: the swinging weapon, or the bow, staff or thrown weapon an arrow, bolt or bomb left, for its direct hit and its splash damage, even if you've switched weapons while it flew. Before, they used whatever you held when it landed, so switching to a sword mid-flight gave the arrow the sword's affixes. A projectile or area effect the game sets up without an item counts as coming from the attack or shot that spawned it; one with no source at all gets no item affixes. A hit from outside the game's own attack, projectile and area code (another mod's) still uses the weapon in hand. **Play-test 2026-11-07 (single player):** life steal on a bow kept working after switching weapons mid-flight (passed); Skadi couldn't be seen (no separate frost numbers in the game); re-tested 2026-11-08 with the devcommands message: stacks count up and follow the bow mid-flight (passed).
- **Affixes that work in this step** (through the game's own stat getters, so vanilla tooltips, combat and repairs see them): enhanced damage (every combat damage type, not chop or pickaxe damage), fire/frost/lightning/poison/spirit damage (a % of the enhanced physical damage), armour, durability, block power, attack stamina (also the tools' "per swing" version), knockback and backstab (raised on the attacker's game when the hit is made), parry bonus.
- **Storage:** `ItemData.m_customData` keys `corruption.rarity`, `corruption.name` (Rares) and `corruption.affixes` (`id=value;prefix+suffix=value`), saved and synced with the item by the game.
- **Unreadable saved affixes (review fix 2026-11-08; passed alone 2026-11-08: `co_affix` refuses NaN and infinity, keeps a finite 500%):** a saved value that isn't a finite number (`NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, or one too big for the game's numbers) makes the item's affixes unreadable: it gives no affix effects at all and the log says so once (`Loot: unreadable affix data on …`). Its saved text is left exactly as it was, so it can be repaired; `co_affix` refuses to add to such an item rather than overwrite it, and refuses a value that isn't a finite number. Finite values are kept as saved, even outside today's table ranges (older rolls, resonant lines).

**How to test:** `co_loot` drops a Rare item of the biome you stand in; `co_loot magic` a Magic one; `co_loot rare SwordBronze` a chosen base item (any prefab in `loot_bases.csv`). Kill starred creatures for natural drops (1 in 50 / 1 in 100 per kill is slow; `co_loot` is the quick way). `co_affix <affix id> [value] [right|left|helmet|chest|legs|cape|utility]` adds one affix from `affixes.csv` to an equipped item (default: the best value, the right-hand item or the shield), to test an affix without rolling for it. `co_own` (testing, 2026-11-08) makes your game run the object you're looking at (a tree, log, stump or rock), the game's own ownership claim, so a two-player test can choose whose game runs what.

**Config settings** (admin-only, `7 - Loot`):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Enabled` | `true` | Creatures drop Magic and Rare items. Off: no new drops; items already found keep their affixes |

**Data tables** (compiled into the DLL; rebuild after editing): `affixes.csv`, `loot_bases.csv` (rerun `tools/make_loot_bases.py` after a new game dump), `loot_rules.csv` (previous-biome chance, drop level, affix-count weights), `rare_names.csv`, `drop_chances.csv`, `drop_modifiers.csv`. `dotnet test` checks all of them, and that `loot_bases.csv` matches the dump.

**Known issues:**
- Tested in single player 2026-10-29/30 (179 tests pass); not yet with several players.
- **First look 2026-10-29 (user's screenshots):** `co_loot` Rares work: "Night Ward" (Rare Leather Trousers: +15% armour, armour 2 → 2.3) and "Skull Song" (Rare Bronze Sword: +37% damage, slash 35 → 48), yellow titles, affix lines with ranges, inactive lines grey. Magic items work too: "Shieldwall Bronze Atgeir", "Seismic Antler Pickaxe" (gathering affix on a pickaxe), "Crackling Club" (+19% of physical as lightning: blunt 12 adds lightning 2), blue names. Still to check: ground hover names, durability, block, repair, upgrade, natural drops, combat affixes.
- **Play-test 2026-10-29 (phone log):** build check, natural drops from kills, combat affixes (no errors), a resonant line and no errors all passed. **Ground hover name failed** (showed the base name): the game localizes the hover text before our patch sees it, so the patch looked for the untranslated name. **Fixed 2026-10-30; re-test passed.** **Upgrading passed 2026-10-30:** a Rare Bronze Sword taken to level 4 kept its name and affixes; its numbers follow the level (slash 53 = 35 + 3×6; spirit 23 = 43% of 53; durability 623 = 350 × 1.78). **Durability and repair passed 2026-10-30.** Every Session 3 step has passed; multiplayer (personal rolls for each player) not tested yet.
- Gear ranges: 16 set from the game data and approved (2026-10-29); max health, stamina, eitr and life steal are drafts by choice; resistances settled 2026-10-30 (fire, frost, poison, lightning 10–50%; all resistances 2–10%). No caps on stacking carry weight, regen or max health/stamina/eitr (user, for now).
- Personal loot was only the separate rolls here; since 2026-11-26 dropped items are locked to their player for 60 s and have loot beams (passed alone 2026-11-26, merged in PR #88): [Personal loot and loot beams](#personal-loot-and-loot-beams).
- **Fixed 2026-11-07 (review; untested in game):** creatures whose corpse (ragdoll) carries their loot, which is most of them, never rolled Magic or Rare: the game turns the creature's own drops off before our roll, so it looked like a "no drops" creature. Now both death paths roll, once per death, on the creature owner's game only; a creature whose drops were already off before it died, players and tamed creatures still don't. Magic and Rare items drop at the creature where it died (its vanilla loot still comes from the corpse when it fades). Read in the 1.0.14 assemblies (the cloud stand-in), not 1.0.21; tested with a stand-in of the game's call order (`DeathLootTests`). **Play-test 2026-11-07 (single player, 1.0.21): passed:** about 5 Magic/Rare items from about 120 one- and two-star greydwarfs, the expected rate (about 3.7% and 4.5% per kill). Multiplayer not tried.
- The tables are compiled in; the design wants the server's copy to be the one everybody uses (later step).
- Knockback and backstab use the weapon in hand when the hit lands; switching weapons while an arrow flies uses the new one.
- Vanilla tooltip lines for knockback, backstab, parry and stamina show the base item's numbers; the affix line shows the bonus.
- Proc affixes (step 4): the "Deathless spent" icon disappears if you take the Deathless item off and doesn't come back when you put it on again until you relog (the cooldown itself still runs). Life steal also returns health from Reprisal's and Wrath's damage (open question). The Wrath and Reprisal visuals are read from Stagbreaker and Dundr at first use; the log line "Proc affixes: Reprisal shows …" names them.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Target | Patch | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `CharacterDrop.OnDeath` | Postfix | Magic and Rare drops and currency orbs ([Currency orb drops](#currency-orb-drops)), rolled per nearby player, once per death, on the creature's owner only; since 2026-11-26 each roll is tagged with its player and each player's boss shards drop here ([Personal loot and loot beams](#personal-loot-and-loot-beams)) |

Embedded and registered as items since 2026-11-08 (see [Riftforge crafting: currency orbs](#riftforge-crafting-currency-orbs)). The orbs keep their Path of Exile names (user, 2026-10-28).

---

## Personal loot and loot beams

*Corruption plugin, ROADMAP L7, [#35](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/35). Krogon's decisions 1–12 of 2026-11-26 (`docs/handoffs/claude-personal-loot-beams-q5glnk.md`). **Tested in-game alone (Krogon's solo play-test on Valheim 1.0.30, 2026-11-26: 14 of 15 steps passed, the first unmarked but its log line there; clean log, every patch applied).** After it the beams' look changed twice (decisions 13–14: half-size tall beams, taper, slow pulse; decision 16: soft edges, glow, smoke), and both passed his final play-tests the same day ("playtest done. ready to finalise."). **Merged into `master` in PR #88 (Corruption 0.10.0).** Not yet tried with two players. Written against Valheim 1.0.14's code and compiled against 1.0.21; his 1.0.30 log confirmed every patch target; his solo play-test is `docs/playtests/claude-personal-loot-beams-q5glnk.md` (steps `pl-1` to `pl-15`, also on the phone page) and the group steps are Session 2, 2l in `docs/PLAYTEST.md`. Design: [`docs/corruption-design.md`](docs/corruption-design.md) → Loot and uniques → Drops.*

**What it does in-game:**
- **A drop rolled for one player is that player's for its first 60 seconds** (decisions 2–5). It carries the player's id (the character's profile id, stable across relogs) and its drop time on the network clock in its ZDO, written by the game that makes the drop (the owner of the creature, rock or heart). For the first 60 s (`personal_loot.csv` → `lock_seconds`) other players **don't see it at all** (the item, its own lights and its beam are hidden on their games; no hover text; their crosshair passes through it to whatever is behind) and **can't pick it up** (E, auto-pickup, any path: it is refused in `Humanoid.Pickup`, and their auto-pickup doesn't pull it or ask for it). Then it appears for everyone and anyone can take it. Logging out or dying changes nothing (decision 3). **No hover text about owners or time, for anyone** (decision 4: "Nothing. Since drops are not visible to the player they dropped for. Once 60 seconds is up, it doesn’t matter, everyone sees it. No owner name needed to display since loot is unlocked.").
- **What is personal** (decision 5): kill loot (each nearby player's Magic/Rare gear, orbs, maps, the corrupted boss's guaranteed orb); each player's boss shard (`BossShardsPerPlayer`, now dropped per player at the kill instead of in the shared drop list); orbs from Hoardfinder's and of Hidden Riches (locked to the striker; the striker's id now travels in the `Corruption_HarvestChances` message). **Shared (no owner):** vanilla loot, boss essences, the first-kill 1–3 and corrupted-boss 3–5 shards, creature shards, dungeon chests (their orbs and shards stay inside the chest). With nobody within the game's nearby range (100 m), the one roll a kill still gets has no owner, as before.
- **Items a player drops from their inventory are never locked and get no beam**, so trading stays free (the tags are on the ground item's ZDO, never in the item data, so they don't travel into inventories).
- **No rip chest any more** (decisions 6 and 9). Destroying the heart spills its loot on the ground around it, on the heart owner's game. Each player near the heart (100 m) keeps their own orb roll and Magic/Rare roll, locked to them. The shared part (the vanilla loot of one of the biome's chests from `rip_loot.csv`, the 1–3 shards `ChestShardsMin`/`ChestShardsMax`, the one map) is dealt one item at a time in turn, players in a random order, the turn carrying on between kinds (10 coins for 3 players go 4/3/3; the next kind starts with the next player), and each player's share is locked to them. Krogon (decision 6): "If an odd number rolls, someone may miss out and that’s fine." **How the shared part is dealt is Claude's reading of decision 6 (decision 9), to confirm in the play-test.** The exit rip is unchanged. Log lines on the heart owner's game: `Rip loot: N player(s) near the heart.`, `Rip loot: player <name> (<id>): k orb(s) (…), g gear (…), shared: … .`, `Rip loot: spilled N drop(s) at …`.
- **Loot beams on every Corruption drop**, personal or shared (decisions 7, 10, 11, 12; Krogon: "All orbs, shards, and magic, rare and unique loot"): a vertical line of light (a Unity `LineRenderer`, `Sprites/Default` shader, colour fading upward) from the item, drawn locally on each game (not saved, not networked), following the item. While a drop is locked to someone else its beam is hidden too; after the lock everyone sees it; it stays until the item is picked up. Vanilla items and essences get no beam.

| Drop | Colour | Beam |
|---|---|---|
| Magic gear | `#8888FF` (blue) | short |
| Rare gear | `#FFFF77` (yellow) | tall |
| Unique gear | `#FFAC00` (orange-gold) | tall (no uniques exist yet; `co_personal drop unique` previews it) |
| Shard | `#B3170D` (red) | short |
| Map | `#18FF00` (green) | tall |
| Transmutation, Augmentation, Alchemy, Regal, Annulment | `#FFFFFF` (white) | short |
| Exalted, Divine, Chance, Mirror | `#FFFFFF` (white) | tall |
| Jeweller's Orb | no row (doesn't drop) | none |

  **Tall** = 5 m high, 0.175 m wide at the base, with a small light (halved after the play-test: Krogon, "shrink to half the size"; it was 10 m and 0.35 m). **Short** = 2.5 m, 0.15 m at the base, no light. **Every beam** narrows from its base to a quarter of that width at the top and pulses slowly, dimming by up to 35% once every 4 s, each drop out of step with its neighbours (Krogon, 2026-11-26: "thick at base and thinner towards the top", "pulsated very slowly"). All in `personal_loot.csv` (`top_width_fraction`, `pulse_seconds`, `pulse_strength`); the shares and the pulse are first guesses. **After the final play-test (decision 16: "they are too solid looking")** each beam is soft-edged and see-through (a generated edge texture fading to nothing at the sides, `edge_softness`; core opacity `beam_alpha` 0.45), has a faint wider glow around it (`glow_width` ×3, `glow_alpha`) and slow smoke-like wisps rising up it (a small particle system per beam: `smoke_per_second`, `smoke_seconds`, `smoke_size`, `smoke_alpha`, `smoke_drift`, `smoke_start_scale`, `smoke_end_scale`, `smoke_fade_in`; short beams' wisps scale with their height). All first guesses, tunable live with `co_personal look`.
- **Sound (decisions 8 and 12):** plays once when a tall-beam drop lands, for its owner only (within 5 s of the drop, `sound_window_seconds`; at most one every 0.3 s so a pile doesn't stack). The common drops are silent. The sound is `default` = the game's skill-level-up sound (only its sound parts), kept for every tall row by Krogon after the play-test (decision 15: "fine with the level up sound for now. maybe we can make our own custom sounds similar to diablo 4 drops sounds"); `co_personal sound` can still try others, and Rare and unique can get different ones.
- **Merging:** vanilla's auto-stack of nearby identical drops (it only runs with more than 200 drops loaded) never merges a tagged drop, so no owner or beam is lost.
- If `personal_loot.csv` or `loot_beams.csv` has an error, the feature turns off (vanilla drops) and the log says why.

**Data:** new tables, both embedded in the DLL: [`Corruption/Data/personal_loot.csv`](Corruption/Data/personal_loot.csv) (settings: `lock_seconds` 60 from the design; `sound_window_seconds` 5, `refresh_seconds` 0.25, beam sizes and lights are first guesses) and [`Corruption/Data/loot_beams.csv`](Corruption/Data/loot_beams.csv) (14 rows: drop, colour, short or tall, sound). `PersonalLootRulesTests` and `LootBeamTableTests` check that the lock is 60 s, the tall and short sets and "sound for tall only" match the decisions, the colours, and that every dropping orb has a row. Pure rules: `Corruption/Rules/PersonalLootRules.cs` (lock state Free/Mine/Theirs, seconds left, dealing, sound rule, merge rule) and `Corruption/Rules/LootBeamTable.cs`. ZDO keys on ground items: `corruption_loot_owner` (long, player id), `corruption_loot_time` (long, network ticks), `corruption_loot_beam` (string, a `loot_beams.csv` row).

**Config:** no new options. `[Rips] ChestShardsMin`/`ChestShardsMax` keep their keys; their descriptions now say "the rip heart spills".

**Network:** the `Corruption_HarvestChances` message now ends with the striker's player id (long), read only when it is there, so a striker on an older build still gets their harvest affixes (their orb is then unowned). Everyone and the server must be on the same build: Corruption 0.10.0 (raised at the merge), so Jötunn, which compares the first two numbers, turns away an older 0.9.0 game (one would show everyone's locked drops and never lock its own).

**Removed:** the rip chest (`RipLoot.SpawnChest` became `RipLoot.Spill`), `OrbDrops.PlacingRipChest` and its check in the `Container.AddDefaultItems` postfix.

**Console (testing, a cheat; type `co_personal` alone for help):**
- `co_personal drop <magic|rare|unique|shard|map|orb ID> [other] [seconds]`: one drop in front of you, yours, or with `other` a made-up other player's (id 1: invisible to you until its lock ends); `seconds` = lock time left.
- `co_personal beams [other]`: one of every beam row in a line 4 m ahead.
- `co_personal list [range]`: every tagged drop within range (default 30 m): beam, owner (you, the test owner, a player id or nobody), seconds of lock left, whether your game shows or hides it, distance.
- `co_personal sounds [filter]`: candidate sounds (found or missing on this game: `default`, `sfx_coins_pile_placed`, `sfx_coins_placed`, `sfx_chest_open`, `sfx_fader_bell`, `sfx_bowl_AddItem`; names are from Jötunn's prefab list generated from Valheim 1.0.7), or every sound prefab whose name contains the filter.
- `co_personal play <sound|default>` and `co_personal sound <row|tall> <sound|default>`: use a sound for a beam row (or all tall rows) until the game quits; it prints the line to send Claude.
- `co_personal look` lists every `personal_loot.csv` setting with its value now; `co_personal look <setting> <value>` changes one until the game quits (same checks as the table) and redraws every beam at once, printing the line to send Claude; `co_personal look reset` goes back to the table's values.

**Multiplayer:** each game works out "locked or not" itself, from the tags in the ZDO and its own copy of the network clock (the server corrects it every 2 s), so the reveal can be a second early or late on one game. Not tried with two players.

**Known issues and limits:**
- Every patch applied on Valheim 1.0.30 in Krogon's play-test (2026-11-26). If a later game version adds an overload to a name-only target, the log says `Failed to apply patch … PersonalLootPatches+<class>`; the worst case is `Awake` (other players would see locked drops).
- On a dedicated server the network clock only runs while someone is online, so a lock pauses while nobody is on. Sleeping through the night moves the clock on, so it ends every running lock at once.
- Creatures can't eat a locked food drop either (the `CanPickup` refusal also covers vanilla `ItemDrop.RemoveOne`); only the heart's dealt vanilla loot could be food.
- The heart's loot is now loose drops pushed outward like kill drops, so they can roll into water or off a ledge.
- On ragdoll deaths each player's boss shard drops at the death; the shared shards drop when the corpse fades (vanilla timing).
- The beams' look passed Krogon's play-tests (2026-11-26) with `personal_loot.csv`'s values as they are; the sound is the game's level-up sound for now (custom drop sounds like Diablo 4's are an idea for later).
- Clients are trusted, as in vanilla (a modified game could ignore the lock).
- Vanilla's own `Player.FindHoverObject` sort changed by 1.0.21 (the cloud's 1.0.21 references have no `RaycastHitComparer`); our replacement ray loop sorts by distance itself and copies 1.0.14's code. Check hovering over a pile.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**

| Class (file) | Target | Kind | Priority | Can skip vanilla |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PersonalLootPatches.Pickup` (`Content/PersonalLootPatches.cs`) | `Humanoid.Pickup(GameObject go, bool autoequip, bool autoPickupDelay)`, matched by name, item read as `__0` | Prefix | `Priority.First` | Yes: `__result = false` when the drop is locked to another player (any error = allow) |
| `PersonalLootPatches.Interact` | `ItemDrop.Interact(Humanoid, bool, bool)` | Prefix | Normal | Yes: `__result = false`, silently, when hidden from the local player |
| `PersonalLootPatches.RequestOwn` | `ItemDrop.RequestOwn()`, matched by name | Prefix | Normal | Yes: skipped for a drop locked to another player (added after the multiplayer review, so the lock doesn't depend only on the hidden view; `ItemDrop.RPC_RequestOwn` stays vanilla, so the lock's owner always gets the item) |
| `PersonalLootPatches.CanPickup` | `ItemDrop.CanPickup(bool autoPickupDelay)`, matched by name | Prefix | Normal | Yes: `__result = false` for a drop locked to another player (auto-pickup then neither pulls nor asks for it; creatures run by other games can't eat it); also ends the game's pickup retry loop (`PickupUpdate`) on its first tick, so it can't hand the item over by itself after the lock |
| `PersonalLootPatches.AutoStack` | `ItemDrop.AutoStackItems()` | Prefix | Normal | Yes: when this drop is tagged (also sets `m_haveAutoStacked`) or a tagged neighbour vanilla would merge (same name, quality, `m_autoStack`, owned, within 4 m) |
| `PersonalLootPatches.FindHover` | `Player.FindHoverObject(out GameObject, out Character)`, args as `__0`/`__1` | Postfix | Normal | No; when vanilla's hover is a drop hidden from this player, re-runs vanilla's ray loop (1.0.14 copy) skipping hidden drops |
| `PersonalLootPatches.Awake` | `ItemDrop.Awake()` | Postfix | Normal | No; starts the view when the ZDO has our keys; inside a creature drop, tags a shard with a shared beam |
| `PersonalLootPatches.CreatureDrops` | `CharacterDrop.DropItems(...)` (static), matched by name only | Prefix + Finalizer | Normal | No; a depth counter |
| `LootHoverText` (`Content/LootPatches.cs`, changed) | `ItemDrop.GetHoverText()` | Postfix | Normal | No; returns "" for a drop hidden from the local player |
| `LootDrops.Rolls` (changed) | `CharacterDrop.OnDeath()` | Postfix | Normal | No; tags each roll with its player; drops each player's boss shards (with the kill's cheated flag) |
| `ShardDrops` (changed) | `CharacterDrop.GenerateDropList()` | Postfix | Normal | No; no longer adds the per-player boss shards |
| `LootHarvest.HarvestDrops` (changed) | `DropTable.GetDropList()` (no arguments) | Postfix | Normal | No; the Hoardfinder's/of Hidden Riches orb is kept out of the list and dropped to the striker |
| `OrbDrops.ChestOrbs` (changed) | `Container.AddDefaultItems()` | Postfix | Normal | No; the rip-chest check is gone |

Plus a local component `PersonalLootView` (no patch) on tagged drops on every game except a dedicated server. Other mods: see [`docs/mod-compatibility.md`](docs/mod-compatibility.md) (AdventureBackpacks 2.4.8, EquipmentAndQuickSlots 3.2.1 and PlantEasily 2.3.1 were decompiled and read for these methods on 2026-11-26: none patches them).

---

## Combat mechanics

**Status:** Planned. Not started.

**Ideas so far:**
- Perfect-parry timing windows, combos, a posture/stagger bar, dodge i-frames and weapon special moves.
- Likely patch points: `Humanoid`, `Attack`, `Character.Damage`.
- New animations need asset-bundle work in the `JotunnModUnity` project. They are allowed where necessary (user, 2026-10-30).

The Corruption mod design (`docs/corruption-design.md`) makes fights harder (maddened creatures, corrupted bosses) without new attacks or animations. These combat ideas remain a separate track.

## Levels and XP

*Orlog (new plugin, `com.krogongames.orlog`). The server and every player need it.*

**What it does:** characters gain XP from kills and level up to 99, Diablo 2 style. Built 2026-10-30; not yet tested in-game.
- **XP needed per level:** Diablo 2's experience table (500 for level 2, 7,875 for level 5 … 3.5 billion for 99), `Orlog/Data/levels.csv`.
- **Creature levels:** bosses have the user's levelling guide level (Eikthyr 5, The Elder 11, Bonemass 20, Moder 35, Yagluth 50, The Queen 62, Fader 76, Kall Fimbulbringer 90, all three phases). Any other creature takes the level range of the **biome it dies in** (Meadows 1–4, Black Forest 5–10, Swamp 11–19, Mountains 20–34, Plains 35–49, Mistlands 50–61, Ashlands 62–75, Deep North 76–89) and is placed in it by its base health on a log scale between the biome's weakest and strongest creature (from the game dump's spawn lists). A draugr is a strong Meadows creature but a weak Swamp one. The ocean has no range: its creatures count as the players' average level.
- **XP per kill:** `Orlog/Data/creature_xp.csv` at the creature's level: D2's XP to the next level divided by **classic WoW's kills per level** (WoW's XP table ÷ level×5+45), so a player needs about as many same-level kills per level as in classic WoW without quests (8 at level 1, about 60 at level 11, 290 at 51, 617 from level 89). At the default pace that is about 60 same-level kills to reach Eikthyr's level, 320 to the Elder's, 1,050 to Bonemass's, 3,200 to Moder's, 6,600 to Yagluth's, 10,600 to the Queen's, 16,800 to Fader's and 24,600 to Kall's (from the tables; real play mixes in weaker and starred creatures). `XpMultiplier` changes the pace.
- **Stars:** a 1-star creature gives ×2, a 2-star ×3 (its health is base × its game level, from the game code).
- **Level difference:** a creature **at or below** your level gives its own XP, cut by Diablo 2's rule when it's far below (within 5 levels full XP; 6–10 below 81% down to 5%). A creature **above** your level gives the XP of a creature of *your* level plus classic WoW's bonus, +5% per level above, at most +20% (changed 2026-10-30 after the play-test, user's choice: D2 paid a higher creature's full XP, which made early levels 2–3× too fast). Level 2 now takes about 8 Meadows greydwarves (was 3), Eikthyr's level about 60 kills of the Meadows' toughest creatures.
- **Sharing:** every player within `ShareRange` (70 m) of the kill shares it, D2 party style: +35% per extra player, split by level, each share taken of what the kill is worth to that player (level difference above). Only kills by a player or a creature on the players' side (tamed, summoned) give XP; tamed creatures and the training dummy give none.
- XP is kept with the character (custom data `orlog_xp`), like vanilla skills, so it carries across worlds. Vanilla skills keep levelling by use. No XP loss on death (user).
- On each kill "+N XP" in the top-left (per-player setting); on a new level "Level N" in the centre with the vanilla skill level-up effect.
- Stat and skill points are counted (5 and 1 per level by default) for the UI, but can't be spent yet (attributes and skills come next).

**How it works:** the creature's owner (the game that ran the fight) works out the XP in a prefix on `Character.OnDeath` and sends each player's share in the routed message `Orlog_Xp` to that player's game. Every game publishes its player's level on the player's ZDO (`orlog_level`) so the owner can split by level and apply the level difference. The UI reads level and XP through `Orlog.Api` (`docs/classes-ui-brief.md`).

**Config settings** (`1 - Levels` admin-only, server-synced; `2 - Display` per player):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `MaxLevel` | `99` | Highest level (D2) |
| `StatPointsPerLevel` | `5` | Attribute points per level (D2) |
| `SkillPointsPerLevel` | `1` | Skill points per level (D2) |
| `XpMultiplier` | `1` | Multiplies all kill XP; 1 = classic WoW's pace without quests |
| `ShareRange` | `70` | Metres within which players share a kill (EpicMMOSystem's group range) |
| `PartyBonusPerPlayer` | `0.35` | Extra XP per additional sharing player (D2) |
| `HighLevelPenalty` | `false` | D2's XP cut from level 70; off because it would put level 90 by Kall out of reach |
| `ShowXpGains` | `true` | "+N XP" per kill |

Console commands: `orlog_info` (level, XP, the biome's creature levels), `orlog_xp <amount>` and `orlog_level <level>` (cheats, need `devcommands`).

The tables, the level difference and party rules are unit-tested (`OrlogLevelTests`: 28 tests).

**Known issues / not yet:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-30 (user, single player):** `orlog_info`, "+N XP" per kill, ×2 XP from a 1-star, level-up message and effect, 6 XP for a boar at level 20 (far below), level and XP kept after relogging, no console errors: all work. Not yet tested: sharing with a friend.
- **Pace too fast (user, 2026-10-30):** 3 greydwarves and 1–2 necks reached level 2. Why: the pace assumed same-level kills, but most Meadows enemies (greylings, greydwarves, skeletons, 20+ health) sit at the top of the Meadows range, level 4 (178 XP), and D2 pays a higher creature's full XP within 5 levels; a neck (level 1) gives 62. **Fixed the same day** (user chose option B): higher creatures now give your own level's XP + WoW's up to +20%; a greydwarf at level 1 gives 71. **Re-tested 2026-10-30: "pace feels better" (user).** `XpMultiplier` stays for fine-tuning.
- D2's party bonus is read as +35% per extra player (Arreat: "+35% when a party member is in the area").
- A kill only counts when the last hit came from a player or a creature on their side; a creature finished by fall damage or another creature gives nothing.
- Modded creatures work (level from the biome they die in); modded bosses aren't in the boss list and count as their biome's creatures.
- **Bosses give several creatures' XP** (Krogon, 2026-11-15: "follow diablo 2 style… I dont want players levelling too fast"; built the same day, untested): ×N an ordinary creature of their level, N from Diablo 2's act bosses (their `Exp` rating ÷ an ordinary monster's median 97, `data/d2-reference/act_bosses.csv`): Eikthyr ×9.4, the Elder ×13.8, Bonemass ×17.8, Moder ×20.6, Yagluth ×25.9, the Queen ×33.7, Fader ×48.9, Kall ×66.6 (`biome_levels.csv` → `boss_xp`). A boss kill alone is about a third of a level at Eikthyr and about a tenth later on. Kall pays all his ×66.6 on the final phase's kill; his first two phases give no XP (Krogon, 2026-11-15; `boss_xp_shares` 0;0;1). Stars, sharing, the level difference and `XpMultiplier` apply as for any kill.
- Kall Fimbulbringer's three phases (`FrozenKing`, `_p2`, `_p3`) each count as a level-90 kill.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**
- `Character.OnDeath` (prefix): works out and sends the kill's XP (owner only; reads `m_lastHit`, `m_health`, the star level).
- `Player.OnSpawned` (postfix): publishes the local player's level on its ZDO.
- `Game.Start` (postfix): registers the `Orlog_Xp` routed message.

## Attributes and class eitr

*Orlog. Built 2026-10-30; tested in-game 2026-10-31 (single player).*

**What it does:** a character with a class spends its stat points (5 per level) on Diablo 2's four attributes, and the class adds health, stamina and an **eitr pool that needs no eitr food**.
- **Class:** only the Amazon is playable (`Orlog/Data/classes.csv`, D2's numbers; Barbarian and Paladin listed as `later`). Until the Orlog stone exists, set it with `orlog_class Amazon` (cheat). No class = vanilla, and points can't be spent.
- **Starting point = vanilla:** a level-1 Amazon with nothing spent has vanilla health, stamina and damage, plus the starting eitr. The D2 starting attributes (Str 20, Dex 25, Vit 20, Energy 15) are shown but their effect is already in Valheim's base values.
- **Every level:** +1 health (D2's +2 life × 0.5), +0.89 stamina (+1 × 0.89), +1.5 eitr.
- **Strength:** +1% physical damage (blunt, slash, pierce) with melee weapons, thrown spears and unarmed, per point (D2: 1% per point).
- **Dexterity:** +1% physical damage with bows and crossbows per point (D2).
- **Vitality:** +1.5 health (D2 Amazon +3 life × 0.5) and +0.89 stamina (+1 × 0.89) per point.
- **Energy:** +1.5 eitr per point (user).
- **Eitr pool (user, 2026-10-30):** 15 at the start, +1.5 per level, +1.5 per Energy point; eitr food adds on top. Vanilla eitr regeneration (5 per second after 1 second without using any, from the game code) refills it, so a class has eitr and regen without eitr food.
- **Scale (proposed, to confirm):** D2 life and stamina are turned into Valheim's by the ratio of the starting values: health 25 / D2 life 50 = 0.5; stamina 75 / D2 stamina 84 = 0.89 (Valheim's starting health and stamina from the game code's player defaults; D2's Amazon). Eitr uses the user's numbers 1:1. Examples: a level-50 Amazon with every point in Vitality has +49 + 367 = +416 health; with every point in Strength, +245% melee damage.
- **Respec:** `orlog_respec` (cheat) for now; the respec item comes later (D2's Token of Absolution, from boss essences with Corruption).

**How to use:** `orlog_class Amazon`, gain levels, then `orlog_stat vitality 5` (or `str`, `dex`, `vit`, `ene`; not a cheat: it stands in for the UI's + buttons). `orlog_info` lists class, attributes, unspent points and every bonus. The UI reads them through `Orlog.Api` (`GetCharacterJson`, `GetAttributeInfoJson`, `SpendStatPoint`).

**Config settings** (`3 - Attributes`, admin-only, server-synced; changes apply at once):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `HealthPerD2Life` | `0.5` | Valheim health per D2 life point |
| `StaminaPerD2Stamina` | `0.89` | Valheim stamina per D2 stamina point |
| `EitrStart` | `15` | Eitr every class starts with |
| `EitrPerLevel` | `1.5` | Eitr per level |
| `EitrPerEnergy` | `1.5` | Eitr per Energy point |
| `DamagePerStrength` | `1` | % physical damage per Strength point (melee, thrown) |
| `DamagePerDexterity` | `1` | % physical damage per Dexterity point (bows, crossbows) |

Unit-tested (`OrlogAttributeTests`: 7 tests).

**Known issues / not yet:**
- **Play-test 2026-10-31 (user, single player, build `claude/class-skills a50272c`, Valheim 1.0.21), all passed:** eitr bar with 15 eitr from the class alone, level and Vitality health/stamina, Energy eitr that refills without eitr food, about 20% more club and bow damage from 20 Strength/Dexterity, respec and `orlog_class none`, class and points kept after relogging, the classes UI showing real values and spending points with its + buttons, no console errors. Noted (UI side): double click sounds when spending points, especially the first click.
- Valheim's starting stamina is read from the game code's default (75); if the player prefab sets another value, the stamina scale changes with it.
- D2's other Dexterity effects (attack rating, defence, block chance) and Strength's item requirements have no Valheim counterpart and are left out. The earlier idea of carry weight from Strength is dropped (not D2).
- The skill points are counted but have nothing to spend on until the skill trees.
- **Damaged saves (review fix 2026-11-08; passed alone 2026-11-08: spending, relog, lowered setting keeps the spent points):** saved point counts too big for the game's whole numbers read as the most there can be (so they still count as spent and nothing more can be spent) instead of 0, and the totals no longer overflow or throw (the character API and the `orlog_info` command used to throw on such a save). Nothing is refunded or removed: as before, a lowered `StatPointsPerLevel` leaves the spent points where they are with nothing more to spend.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**
- `Player.GetTotalFoodValue` (postfix, local player only): adds the class's health, stamina and eitr to the base-plus-food totals the game turns into max health, stamina and eitr.
- `SEMan.ModifyAttack` (postfix, local player's attacks only): multiplies blunt, slash and pierce damage by the Strength or Dexterity bonus.

## Orlog stone and class choice

*Orlog. Built 2026-11-01; play-tested three times on 2026-11-02. It **works**: spot, raven, hover, choosing the Amazon from the window, keeping it after relogging. Its look isn't right yet; the user will redesign it later (2026-11-02). Roadmap task C1.*

**What it does:** a character chooses its class at the **Orlog stone** in the spawn area (`class-design.md` §5a; user, 2026-10-30).
- **Look (user, 2026-11-02):** a copy of the game's **Vegvisir** standing stone with every vanilla behaviour removed (hover, use, lore, trophy stands, ravens). Enlarged to **about twice a boss trophy stone** (`StoneSize` 1.7 × the Vegvisir's own 1.85 m). Its runes glow in the classes UI's rune blue (every glowing material turned blue at its own brightness, plus a soft blue light). No mist (two tries didn't show; left for the redesign). The Vegvisir only exists inside location prefabs, so they're searched once when the world's locations load (the first, by name, that has one; logged). If none has one, the trophy stone is the fallback (logged as a warning).
- **Where (user, 2026-11-02):** **behind the Eikthyr trophy stone**, on the line from the spawn area's centre through it, with **3 m between the two stones** (`StoneGap`, edge to edge from both footprints), on the ground there, turned the way the Eikthyr stone is (`StoneTurn` adds degrees if it faces the wrong way). The Eikthyr stone is the trophy stone whose stand takes the levelling guide's first boss's trophy. The player's game works out the spot; the server confirms no stone exists yet before it's placed; a stone already in the world (from the first version) is moved to the spot.
- **Use (E) without a class:** opens the class choice window of the classes UI (CraftingOverhaul); choosing the Amazon and confirming gives the class ("You walk the path of the Amazon", the level-up effect). The choice only works after using the stone and staying near it (twice the game's interaction reach). Without CraftingOverhaul, using the stone twice within 10 s chooses the only playable class.
- **Use with a class:** says which path you walk. Changing class at the stone comes later (user).
- **Raven (user, 2026-11-02):** on the ground beside the stone, reachable; the first time a player comes near it explains the stone ("The Norns weave a fate for every warrior…"), once per player.
- `orlog_class` stays as a cheat for testing.

**Config settings** (`4 - Orlog Stone`, admin-only, server-synced):

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `StoneSize` | `1.7` | The stone's size in times the Vegvisir it copies (1.85 m); 1.7 ≈ twice a trophy stone (user). Restart to apply |
| `StoneGap` | `3` | Metres between the Eikthyr stone and the Orlog stone (user). Moves it next time a player comes |
| `StoneTurn` | `0` | Degrees added to the stone's turn; 180 if it faces the wrong way |

**How it works:** `OrlogStone.Build` runs on Jötunn's `ZoneManager.OnVanillaLocationsAvailable` (once per game run) and registers the prefab late (`RegisterToZNetScene`). `OrlogStone.Tick` (from the plugin's `Update`, every 2 s) finds the start location's centre (`ZoneSystem.GetLocationIcon` of `Game.m_StartLocation`), the loaded Eikthyr trophy stone and any Orlog stone; with none, it asks the server (`Orlog_PlaceStone`), which checks every object for an `orlog_stone` and answers (`Orlog_PlaceStoneReply`); the asking game instantiates it. Spot maths in the pure, tested `StonePlacement` (behind, distance from footprints). `Orlog.Api.GetClassStoneRequest` and `ChooseClass` answer for real.

Unit-tested (`OrlogStoneTests`: 4 tests: straight behind, any angle, on-centre, gap from footprints).

**Known issues / not yet:**
- **Play-test 2026-11-02 (user, first version), failed, all fixed the same day, to re-test:**
  - **Wrong look and spot:** the trophy stone copy showed the Bonemass carving, touched the Bonemass stone and faced out of the circle. Now: the Vegvisir copy, behind the Eikthyr stone, larger, with blue mist and runes (user's choices).
  - **Wrong hover and lore:** it read "Sacrificial Stone / [E] Read" with the vanilla lore. The copied stone carried the game's lore-stone behaviour, which took the hover and use before ours. Now every vanilla hover/use behaviour is stripped.
  - **Crash:** pressing E once crashed the game. The cause isn't known (no log sent); it was the vanilla lore stone's use, now removed. If it happens again, send `Player-prev.log` (`%USERPROFILE%/AppData/LocalLow/IronGate/Valheim/`).
  - **Raven out of reach:** it sat out of reach on top. Now it's on the ground beside the stone.
- **Re-test 2026-11-02 (user) of the rework:**
  - **Passed:** the build check, the spot behind the Eikthyr stone, the raven on the ground, the hover text, and choosing the Amazon from the window.
  - **About 10× a trophy stone instead of 2×:** the size was measured on the whole trophy stone prefab, its stand and effects included. **Fixed:** both heights now come from each stone's main mesh (the trophy stone's `BossStone.m_mesh`, the Vegvisir's biggest visible mesh), and the log prints both heights and the scale.
  - **No blue mist:** a particle system built from scratch with only the smoke material didn't render. **Fixed:** a copy of one of the game's own looping smoke effects, with its scripts, lights and sounds removed, tinted blue and spread over the footprint (logged).
  - **The game froze on pressing E a second time** (with a class; no crash log). The cause is not found: nothing in the stone's use, the class choice window or the raven loops. **Instrumented:** each step of using the stone is now logged, so the next `LogOutput.log` shows where it stops. Copy it before restarting the game, because BepInEx overwrites it at launch.
- **Third round 2026-11-02 (user):** works. Using the stone opens the class window and choosing the Amazon works (logged), she's kept after relogging, no red errors, **no freeze** (the window stays open, so E can't be pressed again; the second round's freeze wasn't reproduced). Still wrong:
  - **Size:** still 15.5 m. The log showed why: the trophy stone's mesh measures 7.4 m and the Vegvisir 1.8 m, so matching heights made it ×8.4. **Changed:** no measuring; `StoneSize` is now times the Vegvisir's own size, 1.7 by default (from the user's view that 15.5 m looked like about 10 trophy stones, one reads as about 1.55 m; twice that is 3.1 m = 1.7 × 1.85 m). **Passed 2026-11-02 (user): "x2 of normal stone"**; `orlog_class none` then the stone still works.
  - **Mist:** the copied effect was an indoor dust effect, `interior_dust`, and didn't show. **Removed**; the look is left for the redesign (user: "I think we will redesign this in the future").
- `StoneTurn` turns the stone without a rebuild.
- In a fresh world two players arriving at the same moment both ask the server; the server lets only the first place the stone.
- The raven shows once per player, also for a character that already has a class.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):**
- `Game.Start` (postfix): registers the stone's two server messages and resets the placement check for the new world.
- No other patch: the stone is our own object (`Hoverable`, `Interactable`).

## Skill tree and skill points

*Orlog. Built 2026-11-01; tested in-game 2026-11-02 (single player, all 9 steps passed). Roadmap task C2. The passives act since 2026-11-11 ([Amazon passives](#amazon-passives), C3); the other skills come one by one (C5 onwards). **Overhauled 2026-11-15 ([#66](https://github.com/krogon-games/JotunnModStub/issues/66), branch `claude/laughing-mccarthy-fnz0to`): no longer a tree; tested in-game the same day (single player, all 15 steps).***

**What it does:** the Amazon's skills in the classes UI's skills panel, and spending skill points on them. Since the overhaul (Krogon, 2026-11-15: "Skill will not need to branch anymore. Players will be able to learn whichever skill they want even at level 1") a tab is a set of nine skill cards, not a tree; the same goes for every later class.
- **The skills:** `Orlog/Data/amazon_skills.csv`, made by `tools/make_orlog_tables.py`: three tabs of exactly nine (the table and its test refuse any other count), each skill with its place on the three-by-three grid, our status, kind, weapon and Valheim description (`class-design.md` §5), and D2's mana where D2 has the skill (`data/d2-reference/amazon.csv`). 27 skills: 13 built (12 `v1` and Penetrate `reworked`), 14 `later`.
  - **Bow and Crossbow (Krogon's list, 2026-11-15):** Forked Lightning, Wind Lance, Snipe, **Multishot** (Diablo 2's Multiple Shot, renamed by Krogon on 2026-11-15; its id stays `MultipleShot`, so saved points, slots and `orlog_skill MultipleShot` still work), Strafe, **Freezing Arrow**, Immolation Arrow, Venom Strike, Shackle Shot. The two in bold work since the overhaul; **Forked Lightning and Wind Lance were built on 2026-11-16** (untested; `Amazon skill bar and bow skills` below) with the numbers Krogon agreed that day; **Strafe, Immolation Arrow and Venom Strike were built the same day (2026-11-16; Krogon's numbers; tested in-game 2026-11-17, single player)**; Snipe and Shackle Shot were the last two, built on 2026-11-18 (Krogon's numbers; untested; both 9 + 0.25 eitr per level), so no bow skill shows "Coming later" any more (Venom Strike's eitr is 7 + 0.5 per level since 2026-11-16). Removed: Magic Arrow, Fire Arrow, Cold Arrow, Exploding Arrow, Ice Arrow, Guided Arrow ("spending a skill point should change how you use the bow, especially when ordinary ammunition already supplies fire and frost damage").
  - **Passive and Magic:** Inner Sight, Critical Strike, Penetrate, Dodge, Avoid, Evade (all six work), Decoy, Valkyrie, Pierce (later). Removed: Slow Missiles.
  - **Javelin and Spear:** Jab, Impale, Fend, Charged Strike, Lightning Strike, Lightning Bolt, Poison Javelin, Plague Javelin, Lightning Fury (none acts yet: roadmap C6, where the list can still change). Removed: Power Strike.
  - **Points in a removed skill are handed back:** a character saved before the overhaul gets them as unspent points the first time it loads (they simply stop counting; the next point spent saves without them), and a skill slot holding a removed skill reads as empty.
- **Points:** 1 per level (from level 2, D2), at most 20 per skill (`MaxSkillPoints`). **No level tiers and no prerequisites:** any built skill takes a point at level 1. D2's pace (`SkillPace`) still applies, now counted from level 1: a skill holds at most as many points as the character has levels (2 points at level 2, 10 at level 10). Later skills can't take points ("Coming later"). Refusals give their reason ("Needs level 4", "Maxed", "No skill points").
- **Eitr cost** shown per skill: D2's mana at the skill's level (Multiple Shot 4, +1 per level; Freezing Arrow 9, +0.25 per level). The class eitr pool is D2's mana 1:1, so D2's costs keep D2's balance.
- Saved with the character (custom data `orlog_skills`). `orlog_respec` returns skill points with the stat points. `orlog_skill <id> [points]` spends from the console.
- `Orlog.Api` now answers `GetSkillIdsJson`, `GetSkillInfoJson` (every brief field except `reqLevel` and `prereqs`, gone with the tiers on 2026-11-15; `row` 1–3 and `col` 1–3 are the card's place; description and next level text, with the chance for passives since 2026-11-11; the effective level is the hard points until +skills exist) and `SpendSkillPoint`; `GetCharacterJson`'s `skillPoints` is the unspent count.
- New reference data for the next steps: `data/d2-reference/amazon_levels.csv`, every Amazon skill's values per level 1–20 (Arreat Summit and D2R, differences noted in its README).

Unit-tested (`OrlogSkillTests`: 11 tests: the real table and its nine per tab, kinds and weapons, any built skill at level 1, later skills refused, the pace, cap and points, removed skills' points handed back, eitr costs, saving, bad tables).

**Known issues / not yet:**
- **The overhaul passed Krogon's play-test (2026-11-15, single player, all 15 steps, no notes):** an old character's points in removed skills handed back, the nine cards per tab, Freezing Arrow learned at level 2, the refusals, a maxed skill, dragging to slots, Multiple Shot, Freezing Arrow, Inner Sight and a passive still working, no errors. Checklist: `docs/playtests/claude-laughing-mccarthy-fnz0to.md`. Two players: not tried (nothing new to try).
- **Seven of the nine bow skills don't work yet** (Forked Lightning, Wind Lance, Snipe, Strafe, Immolation Arrow, Venom Strike, Shackle Shot): each needs its details agreed with Krogon first. **Update 2026-11-16: Forked Lightning, Wind Lance, Strafe, Immolation Arrow and Venom Strike were built that day (Strafe, Immolation Arrow and Venom Strike tested in-game 2026-11-17, single player); only Snipe and Shackle Shot still say "Coming later". Update 2026-11-17: Strafe remade as rapid fire (bows and crossbows), tested in-game the same day (single player).**
- **Before the overhaul**, play-test 2026-11-02 (user): every step passed: the real tree in three tabs, later skills greyed, "Needs Magic Arrow", Dodge holding 7 at level 12, Exploding Arrow's two prerequisites, tooltips with eitr cost, respec, saving, `orlog_skill`, no errors.
- Points go into skills that don't act yet, apart from the passives (2026-11-11); each other skill's effect (and its Valheim numbers, to agree with the user) comes with roadmap C5–C7.
- No +skills from items yet (the loot session's affixes), so the effective level equals the points.
- **Damaged saves (review fix 2026-11-08; passed alone 2026-11-08):** the same overflow-safe totals as the attributes: a huge saved count reads as the most there can be and blocks further spending, without throwing, refunding or removing points; the spending check's "Needs level" doesn't wrap.

**Vanilla behaviour overridden (Harmony patch targets):** none (data, rules and the API only).

## Amazon passives

*Orlog. Built 2026-11-11; **tested in single player 2026-11-15** (Krogon: all 12 steps passed, Moder's breath and the troll's slam included as optional steps 10); multiplayer in `PLAYTEST.md` → 2j. Roadmap task C3 (issue #32); every rule below is Krogon's decision of 2026-11-11 (handoff `docs/handoffs/claude-lucid-albattani-imrei7.md`).*

**What it does:** the first Amazon skills that act. Each has a chance per skill level, Diablo 2's unchanged (`Orlog/Data/amazon_passives.csv`, made by `tools/make_orlog_tables.py` from `data/d2-reference/amazon_levels.csv`); the skills panel's tooltip shows the chance now and at the next level.

| Skill | Chance (level 1 → 20) | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| Critical Strike | 16% → 68% | The hit's physical damage (blunt, slash, pierce) is doubled; fire, frost, poison, lightning and spirit aren't. Any weapon, against creatures only (not players; trees, rocks and buildings are never affected). Plays the game's own critical-hit effect (the one a staggered creature's hit plays) |
| Dodge | 18% → 56% | A creature's close-range hit misses, while you stand still or attack |
| Avoid | 24% → 65% | A creature's projectile misses, while you stand still or attack |
| Evade | 18% → 56% | Any creature hit (close-range or projectile) misses, while you walk or run (moving, not attacking) |
| Penetrate | 16% → 68% (Critical Strike's curve; D2's Penetrate raises attack rating, which Valheim lacks) | The hit ignores the creature's resistances: slightly resistant (¾), resistant (½) and very resistant (¼) become full damage. Immunity stays; weaknesses stay |
